* TODO Nothing asserts a slice carries :COOKIE_DATA:, and its absence only shows once members nest
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       b6da3480-d3ae-4af9-8475-9f0e7c99bb17
:CREATED:  [2026-09-04 Fri 08:57]
:CATEGORY: Slices
:END:
:PLAN:

=.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= says each slice carries =:COOKIE_DATA: checkbox recursive= "so its
statistics cookie counts nested members as well as top-level ones". **Nothing checks it.**
=bin/lint-org= and =config.el= contain zero references to the property — the cookie rules assert a
cookie is *present*, never that it counts the right things.

*Caught 2026-09-04 by the cookie being wrong, not by a check.* =ff7ccb2d= lacked the property. It did
not matter while every member was top-level, and the moment it acquired nested members the headline
recomputed to =[0/9]= against **twelve** checkboxes — the three indented ones silently excluded. The
hand-written =[0/12]= had been right; org's own recount made it wrong, which is the worst direction
for a defect to arrive from, since the recount is what you would trust.

*Surveyed on discovery: 2 of 7 slices lacked it.* =ff7ccb2d= (bitten) and =8a2eb687= (latent — no
nested members yet, so identically broken and invisible). Both set by hand the same day; the survey
is the reason to believe the remaining five are fine rather than merely unexamined.

*The check is cheap and the shape is settled:* a heading with =:KIND: slice= and no
=:COOKIE_DATA:= is an error, in the same clause family as the existing cookie-less-slice and
hybrid rules. Worth adding **now** rather than after the next nesting, which is the argument
=dca940c1= made for landing a rule while instances are still few.

*The sharper question this raises, and it is not settled here:* the property is required by a
convention, defaulted by nothing, and consulted only by org. Every slice needs it and no slice
creates it — =claude-code-ide-org-refresh-slice= already inserts a missing =[/]= cookie for exactly
this reason (=28415ca8=, after relying on the creator to type it failed on the second slice ever
written). The same argument applies here, and a lint error plus a hand fix is the weaker half of it.

:END:
*Verified, not just implemented:* shipped in [[orgit-rev:claude-code-ide-org::5547efe][5547efe]].
Five assertions, run red first — no property at all, the correct value, =:COOKIE_DATA: todo=,
=:COOKIE_DATA: checkbox= without =recursive=, and a non-slice heading that this rule must ignore.
543/543, =bin/lint-org= 0 errors, =bin/check-conventions= 27 ok.

*The value is asserted, not presence*, which was a decision rather than thoroughness. =todo= is a
well-formed value that counts TODO children — of which a slice has none by definition — so a
presence-only rule would accept a slice whose cookie can only ever read =[0/0]=. And =checkbox=
without =recursive= is precisely the case that bit: top-level members count, nested ones vanish.

*Proved to reach the real path, not only the fixture.* The property was deleted from =ff7ccb2d= in
=TODO.org= itself and lint reported
=TODO.org:588: slice has no :COOKIE_DATA: ... (have: nothing)=, after which the file was restored
byte-identical from a copy taken first. That step exists because =542924c1= collects checks that pass
without ever taking the path they claim to test.

*And the first attempt at that step silently mutated nothing* — its anchor assumed =:COOKIE_DATA:=
preceded =:BLOCKER:=, where =org-entry-put= appends after it — so lint found no error for the honest
reason that the file was unchanged. The =assert= printed and the run was discarded. Without it the
output would have read as "rule does not reach the real path", which is a *stronger* and entirely
false conclusion. Worth recording as its own instance: a mutation test needs its mutation asserted,
or it reports on a file it never touched.

*Landed at zero violations.* Both instances were fixed an hour before, so this is =dca940c1='s move —
a rule added while nothing violates it is cheapest and cannot be argued with.

*Left undone, deliberately:* nothing yet *creates* the property. The body above argues that
=refresh-slice= should insert it the way =28415ca8= taught it to insert a missing =[/]=, and this
commit does not do that — a lint error plus a hand fix is the weaker half. Filed as the open question
it was, not closed by implication.


* TODO Nothing lays out future work on a timeline the agenda can show
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       ecf66d45-18b3-477f-bca8-08bec90604e8
:CREATED:  [2026-09-04 Fri 08:52]
:CATEGORY: Skill
:END:

*Wanted by the user 2026-09-04, stated as a requirement for future projects rather than a gap in this
one:* "In future projects I will want to be able to lay out a timeline into the future visible in the
agenda."

*Measured on arrival, because the answer is starker than expected.* Across =TODO.org= and =DONE.org=:
**3 =SCHEDULED:= lines and 0 =DEADLINE:= lines.** All three schedule *recurrences*, not work — the
daily archive and day-node ceremony tasks (=++1d=) and =96a51c2f='s monthly re-measure (=+1m=).
**Not one heading in this project has ever been scheduled to say when it will be done.** Sequencing
is carried entirely by slices, which order work but say nothing about *when*, and by =:BLOCKER:=,
which says what must precede what but not on which day.

So the agenda shows this project's recurrences and nothing else. A forward view — what lands this
week, what slips — has no input.

*What makes this more than "start using =SCHEDULED:=".* The active/inactive timestamp distinction is
already load-bearing here and points the other way: =b8e6007a= settled that the queue records agent
activity and the agenda must not absorb it, which is why span timestamps are inactive. An agenda that
starts carrying *forecasts* has to keep them apart from the *record* — a scheduled date is a claim
about the future and a CLOCK line is a measurement of the past, and the agenda renders both. Deciding
that boundary before populating it is the work, not typing dates.

*Two open questions, neither settled here.* Whether a slice gains dates at all, or only its members —
a slice sequences without committing to a calendar, and dating one may be the wrong shape. And
whether this is a convention for *this* repo or belongs in the portable half, since the ask was
framed as "future projects": that is =9ae4b17e='s classification question arriving from a new
direction, and it is why this carries =:CATEGORY: Skill= rather than =Meta=.

* TODO Accepting a slice proposal leaves its body still calling itself a proposal
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       198dd00e-0746-444a-ab25-2034a2a235c2
:CREATED:  [2026-09-03 Thu 15:49]
:CATEGORY: Slices
:END:

=.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= says accepting a proposal is three mechanical steps: add
=:KIND: slice=, run =claude-code-ide-org-refresh-slice= so the blocker and cookie appear, and link
the prompt revision that picks it up. **None of the three touches the body**, and a proposal's body
is required to say it is a proposal — the withheld declaration is the whole of what a proposal *is*,
so the composer writes that sentence on purpose.

The result is a body that contradicts its own heading from the moment of acceptance. Found on
=ff7ccb2d= 2026-09-03: it opened "*A proposal, not a slice* — the declaration is withheld
deliberately … no =:KIND: slice=, no prompt link, and =MAYBE= rather than =TODO=", while carrying
=:KIND: slice= and having been =NEXT= since **eleven minutes** after it was written. The user caught
it by reading, not by any check.

*Two things to decide, and they are separable.* Whether acceptance grows a fourth step that rewrites
or deletes the proposal paragraph — cheap, but it is prose, so it is judgement rather than mechanism.
And whether =bin/lint-org= can catch the residue instead: a heading with =:KIND: slice= whose body
matches something like "a proposal, not a slice" is a mechanical contradiction, and this project's
precedent is to add such a rule while instances are still few.

*Note the third acceptance step also silently did not happen* on =ff7ccb2d= — it carries no
=orgit-rev:= prompt link at all. So two of three steps were skipped and nothing said so, which
suggests the real fix may be that acceptance is one command rather than a list someone follows.

*=ff7ccb2d='s two instances were repaired by hand the same day, which is evidence for this heading
rather than against it.* Its stale proposal paragraph was rewritten (=d3f08b3=) and its missing
=orgit-rev:= prompt link added (=bc198a0=) — but both took a human noticing, an hour apart, and
neither would have been noticed by anything that runs. The heading stands: the question is not
whether one slice can be fixed, it is that acceptance leaves every slice needing it.


* REVIEW [5/5] Close the four defects the PR #9 review found, and the gap they shared
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       887eaba3-96c5-4821-88e0-7cf60859a9c4
:CREATED:  [2026-09-03 Thu 14:42]
:CATEGORY: Tools
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-09-03 Thu 14:45] \\
  starting the PR9 fixes
- State "REVIEW"     from "DOING"      [2026-09-03 Thu 15:01] \\
  four fixes shipped; threads and push are the user's call
- State "DOING"      from "REVIEW"     [2026-09-03 Thu 15:20] \\
  rework: the guard set was incomplete
- State "REVIEW"     from "DOING"      [2026-09-03 Thu 15:24] \\
  five members shipped; push is the user's call
:END:
:PLAN:

A =/code-review --comment= pass over [[https://github.com/nbest937/claude-code-ide-org.el/pull/9][PR #9]] filed four inline comments, each a defect in
code that PR introduced: two in the new slice guards on =claude-code-ide-org-refile= and
=claude-code-ide-org-capture=, two in =bin/hooks/clock-target-check=. They group because they
arrived together against one unmerged branch and are being fixed on it, not because they share a
mechanism — the two pairs are independent, and the children carry different =:CATEGORY:= values
accordingly.

The two guard defects do share a root cause worth naming once here: the guards ask a *direct*
question (is this heading keyworded, is this target a slice) while the invariant they exist to
protect — =claude-code-ide-org--container-heading-p= as used by the hybrid lint rule — is
*descendant*-based. The same PR's =claude-code-ide-org-set-property= guard already asks the
descendant question in the declaring direction, so the inconsistency is internal to one branch.

Each child names its comment permalink. The threads stay open until the fixes land.

:END:
*Verified, not just implemented:* all four fixed and committed on =feature/fix-claude-md-and-slice-conventions=,
PR #9's own branch — the same integration point, so no new branch. Four commits:
[[orgit-rev:claude-code-ide-org::0b99eaf][0b99eaf]] (both guards),
[[orgit-rev:claude-code-ide-org::3eed7cc][3eed7cc]] (the jq slurp, plus the hook's missing test
harness), [[orgit-rev:claude-code-ide-org::8906742][8906742]] (the sentinel). Closing battery:
=bin/test= 541/541 (was 538), =bin/clock-target-check-test= 12/12 — a file that did not exist —
=bin/lint-org= 0 errors, =bin/check-conventions= 27 ok, and every other shell suite green except
=bin/pre-commit-test=, whose three failures are filed as =0fa403f4= and reproduce identically at
=8c52cd6=.

*Every fix was run red first*, and by copying the file aside and restoring =HEAD='s version in place
rather than stashing. The guard tests failed with =Refiled: "Test heading" under "a note under the
slice"= — the escape itself, not a proxy. The sentinel test was written a commit early and held back
rather than committed red.

*What the review found that the suites could not.* All three PR #9 guards were written in one pass,
and the =org_set_property= one asked =--container-heading-p= while the other two asked direct
questions — an inconsistency internal to a single branch, with 538 green tests over it. The reason it
survived is that the refile guard's negative case was a **childless** keyword-less note, which is
legal for two independent reasons at once; nothing distinguished them until a case was written where
they came apart.

*Left open, deliberately:* the four PR threads are unanswered and unresolved, and the branch is
unpushed. Both are the user's call — replying to a thread publishes, and =.githooks/pre-push= is a
separate gate.

*One repair outside the repo, recorded because nothing else records it:*
=~/.claude/plans/twinkling-meandering-graham.md= vanished mid-session, breaking a =DONE.org= plan link
and blocking a commit through =bin/lint-org=. Restored byte-for-byte from =plans/=, which is exactly
what that archive exists for. Nothing in this session deleted it, and what did is unknown.

*A second piece of residue, filed after the close and worth recording as a miss rather than a
tidy-up:* =dc753eb2= — =org_set_todo= reaches the same hybrid by a third path. Queue a keyword onto
a keyword-less note that already sits under a slice, apply, and the slice has keyworded descendants;
both fixes here guard *arrival*, and nothing guards the grant. It was noticed while implementing
=15847e0b= and then not filed, which is precisely the twin asymmetry CLAUDE.md describes — invisible
from inside the act that creates it, caught by a reader. Its fix is a different shape (a queued tool
has no call-time moment to refuse in), so it is its own heading rather than a fifth member.

*Reopened and closed again, which is worth recording as a shape rather than an incident.* The
paragraph above concluded that =dc753eb2= was "its own heading rather than a fifth member", on the
premise that its fix was open design. The premise was wrong — =claude-code-ide-org-set-todo='s own
docstring already named =org-blocker-hook= as where such a refusal belongs — so the heading was
refiled in as the fifth member, the parent went =REVIEW= → =DOING=, and it shipped in
[[orgit-rev:claude-code-ide-org::dbe2b09][dbe2b09]]. That is exactly what the =REVIEW= → =DOING= row
is for: the review found something missing. The earlier paragraph is left standing, since a debrief
that quietly agrees with itself afterwards is worth less than one that shows where it turned.

**Membership was the question, and the test that settled it was the story's own title.** Three of the
five are defects in code PR #9 introduced; =dc753eb2= is the same feature shipping two-thirds
complete, which is a defect in what PR #9 introduced even though the file it touches is older. What
stayed *out* is =0fa403f4=: it reproduces identically at =8c52cd6=, so it is =origin/main='s defect,
and folding a main-branch fix into a feature PR would make this title false in the other direction.

*Closing battery, all five members landed:* =bin/test= **542/542** (was 538 at the branch point),
=bin/clock-target-check-test= **12/12** — a file that did not exist — =bin/lint-org= 0 errors,
=bin/check-conventions= 27 ok. Five commits of code and fixes, each one red-first.


** DONE A refile guard that asks about the moved heading cannot see the subtree it moves
CLOSED: [2026-09-03 Thu 14:53]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       15847e0b-171a-4aa2-9f3e-e2373779b4c3
:CREATED:  [2026-09-03 Thu 14:42]
:CATEGORY: Tools
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-09-03 Thu 14:45]--[2026-09-03 Thu 14:53] =>  0:08
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-09-03 Thu 14:45] \\
  extract the shared predicate, fix the refile guard
- [2026-09-03 Thu 14:45]--[2026-09-03 Thu 14:53] extract shared predicate, fix refile guard
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-09-03 Thu 14:53] \\
  predicates shipped, three tests run red first
:END:
:PLAN:

[[https://github.com/nbest937/claude-code-ide-org.el/pull/9#discussion_r3927960099][PR #9 comment]]. =claude-code-ide-org-refile= refuses a keyworded arrival under a slice by
testing =org-get-todo-state= on the *moved heading*, but the invariant it protects —
=claude-code-ide-org--container-heading-p=, which the hybrid lint rule uses — scans *descendants*.
So a keyword-less note carrying a =TODO= child refiles straight through and mints the hybrid
=dca940c1= exists to prevent. A second path through the same mismatch: the guard inspects only the
immediate target, so refiling a keyworded heading under a keyword-less *child* of a slice is also
let through.

Reproduced in batch on this branch; both leave =SLICE-P: t CONTAINER-P: t= on the slice. The tool
writes first and =.githooks/pre-commit= gates on the lint, so the refusal arrives at the *next*
commit with the damage already on disk and no tool to undo it.

The fix builds the shared predicate the sibling capture heading then reuses: refuse when the
arriving subtree contains any keyworded heading *and* the target is a slice or lies within one.
The accompanying test uses a childless note as its negative case, which is why the suite is green;
that gap is part of this heading.

:END:
*Verified, not just implemented:* three new ERT tests, each **run red first** against the unfixed
guards before the fix existed — the failing assertion printed
=Refiled: "Test heading" under "a note under the slice"=, which is the escape itself rather than a
proxy for it. 541/541 after, up from 538.

Shipped in [[orgit-rev:claude-code-ide-org::0b99eaf][0b99eaf]] as two predicates rather than a patch
to the guard, so the capture sibling and any later caller ask one question:

- =claude-code-ide-org--subtree-has-keyworded-heading-p= — the arrival side. Differs from
  =claude-code-ide-org--container-heading-p= by exactly the root, which is the difference that
  mattered: the old guard read the heading line only.
- =claude-code-ide-org--enclosing-slice-title= — the target side, walking up with
  =org-up-heading-safe= as =bin/lint-org='s completable-ancestor rule does. It returns a *title*
  because both callers want it only to name the slice in the refusal.

*Not =:KIND:= inheritance, and the docstring says so at length* — =--slice-p= refuses inheritance on
purpose, and the question here is not "is this heading a slice" but "would an ancestor slice acquire
keyworded children". Since =--container-heading-p= scans descendants at any depth, an ancestor at any
depth is the honest scope.

*What differed from the plan:* the refusal messages had to change, not just the predicates. Both now
name the slice they would hybridise rather than the id passed as =target= — which is the only
externally visible evidence the ancestor walk ran, and is what the new arrival-inside test asserts,
with a =should-not= on the target's own title beside it. The two pre-existing message assertions were
updated to the new wording rather than relaxed.

*The named test gap is closed the way it was named:* the old negative case was a *childless*
keyword-less note, which is why the suite stayed green over both holes. That case is kept — it is
still legal — now sitting beside a note that is keyword-less and *not* childless.


** DONE A capture guard that asks whether the target is a slice misses everything inside one
CLOSED: [2026-09-03 Thu 14:53]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       32742646-391c-44e7-b902-d69e51589e59
:CREATED:  [2026-09-03 Thu 14:42]
:CATEGORY: Tools
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "DONE"       from "TODO"       [2026-09-03 Thu 14:53] \\
  target-side widening, no separate clock
:END:
:PLAN:

[[https://github.com/nbest937/claude-code-ide-org.el/pull/9#discussion_r3927962113][PR #9 comment]]. The same predicate mismatch reached through =claude-code-ide-org-capture=: its
clause fires only when =target= *is itself* a slice, while =claude-code-ide-org--container-heading-p=
matches a keyworded descendant at any depth. Capturing with an =initial_state= under a keyword-less
*child* of a slice therefore mints the hybrid unrefused, which falsifies the guard's own comment —
"guarded at the call so the refusal lands before the heading exists" holds only for a direct child.

Only the target side needs widening here, unlike the refile sibling: a capture creates a leaf, so
there is no arriving subtree to scan. Depends on the predicate that heading extracts.

:END:
*Verified, not just implemented:* =claude-code-ide-org-test-capture-refuses-a-keyworded-capture-inside-a-slice=,
run red first — the unfixed guard returned =Captured:= for a =TODO= capture under a slice's note
child. It asserts the refusal names the enclosing slice by title, so a guard that merely errored for
some other reason would not satisfy it. The keyword-less capture beside it still succeeds, which is
the edge the convention keeps legal. 541/541.

Shipped in the same commit as its sibling, [[orgit-rev:claude-code-ide-org::0b99eaf][0b99eaf]], and
**carries no clock of its own**: the whole of the work was
=claude-code-ide-org--enclosing-slice-title=, which =15847e0b= built, plus hoisting a =target-slice=
binding into =claude-code-ide-org-capture='s existing =let*= so the refusal can name the slice
without resolving the marker twice. Recording a separate interval for that would invent one.

*What differed:* nothing about the diagnosis. The one thing worth writing down is that the guard's own
comment had to be corrected rather than extended — it claimed "the refusal lands before the heading
exists", which was true only for a direct child, and a comment that overstates a guard is how the
next reader stops checking.


** DONE One malformed queue line makes clock-target-check nag a session that did clock in
CLOSED: [2026-09-03 Thu 14:57]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       b02191d6-3154-4796-8e4b-a9494769fa5a
:CREATED:  [2026-09-03 Thu 14:43]
:CATEGORY: Dev
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-09-03 Thu 14:53]--[2026-09-03 Thu 14:57] =>  0:04
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-09-03 Thu 14:53] \\
  make the clock_in scan tolerate a bad line
- [2026-09-03 Thu 14:53]--[2026-09-03 Thu 14:57] fix the jq slurp in clock-target-check
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-09-03 Thu 14:57] \\
  fromjson? tolerance plus the missing test harness
:END:
:PLAN:

[[https://github.com/nbest937/claude-code-ide-org.el/pull/9#discussion_r3927964800][PR #9 comment]]. =bin/hooks/clock-target-check= tests for a queued =clock_in= with =jq -e -s=,
which slurps the whole =.jsonl= and parses it as one document — so a single truncated or interleaved
line makes jq exit non-zero, the =if= fails, and the script proceeds as though the session had *no*
=clock_in= at all. It then blocks the Stop and tells a session that did clock in that its time will
reach review UNASSIGNED.

Reachable rather than theoretical: =bin/hooks/queue-append= embeds the entire hook payload per line
via =--argjson=, so a line carrying a large =org_amend= text can exceed the atomic-append size while
three hooks append to one file concurrently.

What makes it look unintended rather than a trade is that the write-evidence scan in the same script
already uses =fromjson?= precisely so malformed lines are skipped. The fix is the same tolerance,
which costs nothing here.

:END:
*Verified, not just implemented:* measured on three fixtures before the edit and again after —
a queue holding a real =clock_in= plus one truncated line exits **5** under =jq -e -s= and **0**
under the replacement, while a queue with no =clock_in= exits **1** under both. So the fix restores
the intended answer without widening what counts as a clock. Shipped in
[[orgit-rev:claude-code-ide-org::3eed7cc][3eed7cc]] as =-n -R + inputs + fromjson?=, which is the
tolerance the write-evidence scan thirty lines below already used deliberately.

*The larger finding is that the hook shipped with no test at all*, which is how both of this
story's hook defects reached a review pass instead of a suite. =bin/clock-target-check-test= now
exists: eleven checks over the clock-evidence scan, the write-evidence scan's project scoping, the
sentinel round trip and the blocking message.

*The discrimination was proved, not assumed*, and by copying the fixed hook aside and restoring
=HEAD='s version in place rather than stashing: the malformed-line check fails against the unfixed
hook and passes against the fixed one. Two assertions were written knowing they would fail — the
second belongs to =ae4e4b04= and was held back rather than committed red.

*Carried along, because a comment is the only place it was recorded:* the suite now pins the reason
the scan is =jq= and not =grep= — an amend body quoting the literal ="kind":"clock_in"= must not
count as a clock. That was a header sentence with nothing enforcing it.


** DONE The once-per-session guard fires anyway when it cannot arm itself
CLOSED: [2026-09-03 Thu 15:00]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       ae4e4b04-e4d0-4ce9-b37f-d1c5479e8735
:CREATED:  [2026-09-03 Thu 14:43]
:CATEGORY: Dev
:END:

- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-09-03 Thu 14:53] \\
  nominated: the sentinel fail-open fix
CLOCK: [2026-09-03 Thu 14:57]--[2026-09-03 Thu 15:00] =>  0:03
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-09-03 Thu 14:57] \\
  make the sentinel guard fail open
- [2026-09-03 Thu 14:57]--[2026-09-03 Thu 15:00] sentinel fail-open fix and its regression test
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-09-03 Thu 15:00] \\
  fail-open sentinel, header corrected, test restored
:END:
:PLAN:

[[https://github.com/nbest937/claude-code-ide-org.el/pull/9#discussion_r3927964971][PR #9 comment]]. =bin/hooks/clock-target-check= writes a sentinel so its reminder fires at most
once per session, but the write discards its own failure and the =exit 2= runs unconditionally — so
an unwritable or full queue directory turns a one-shot reminder into a Stop that blocks every turn.
Both the script header and the message shown to the user assert the guarantee the code does not
enforce, and nothing consults =stop_hook_active= as a second line of defence.

The fix keeps the file's existing fail-open posture: a check that cannot arm its own guard should not
fire.

:END:
*Verified, not just implemented:* the regression test was written alongside =b02191d6='s suite and
deliberately **held back rather than committed red** — it fails against the unfixed hook *and*
against the fix-1-only hook, and passes here, so it discriminates on this defect specifically rather
than on the file having changed. It makes the queue directory unwritable, runs the hook, and asserts
exit 0. Twelve checks green; =bin/test= 541/541; =bin/lint-org= 0 errors;
=bin/check-conventions= 27 ok.

Shipped in [[orgit-rev:claude-code-ide-org::8906742][8906742]] as =: > "$sentinel" 2>/dev/null || exit 0=
— the same fail-open posture as the =command -v jq= and empty-=session_id= exits at the top of the
file. A discipline reminder that cannot bound itself is worse than no reminder.

*The header was corrected in the same commit, not left standing.* Both the LOOP GUARD paragraph and
the "This check fires once per session." sentence shown to the user asserted a bound the code did not
keep; a comment that overstates a guard is how the next reader stops checking. The paragraph now says
the bound is enforced and points at the write.

*Residue, filed rather than described:* =0fa403f4= — =bin/pre-commit-test= reports 5 passed, 3 failed,
and the same three fail identically in a detached worktree at =8c52cd6=, so =origin/main= is shipping
a red suite. Out of this story's scope; not left in prose.


** DONE org_set_todo mints the slice hybrid the other two guards now refuse
CLOSED: [2026-09-03 Thu 15:24]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       dc753eb2-afb5-41d1-b420-9344aac5b662
:CREATED:  [2026-09-03 Thu 15:03]
:CATEGORY: Tools
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-09-03 Thu 15:21]--[2026-09-03 Thu 15:24] =>  0:03
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-09-03 Thu 15:21] \\
  guard the grant on org-blocker-hook
- [2026-09-03 Thu 15:21]--[2026-09-03 Thu 15:24] blocker-hook guard for the keyword grant
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-09-03 Thu 15:24] \\
  grant guarded on org-blocker-hook, four cases red first
:END:
:PLAN:

A keyword-less note under a slice is legal — the container predicate tests keywords. Queue
=org_set_todo TODO= on that note, let a human apply, and the slice has a keyworded descendant: the
same hybrid =dca940c1= forbids and =bin/lint-org= errors on, reached by a third path that neither
=15847e0b= nor =32742646= touches. Both of those fixed *arrival* — refile and capture — and nothing
checks at the moment a keyword is granted to a heading already in place.

*This is the twin the composer could not see.* It was noticed while implementing the other two and
then not filed, which is exactly the asymmetry CLAUDE.md names: scheduling one and forgetting the
other, invisible from inside the act that creates it and caught only by a reader. Filed at close on
the reader's prompt rather than the composer's.

The fix is not obviously the same shape as the other two, which is why this is its own heading. The
guard cannot sit where the others do: =org_set_todo= queues and changes nothing, so refusing at call
time would refuse a keyword that may never be applied, while refusing at apply time means blocking a
human mid-pass over a condition they can see. Deciding between those — or accepting that the lint is
the right net for this one path, since apply is human-triggered and a human is present — is the work.

Note =org_divide= is *not* a fourth path: dividing a note under a slice introduces no keyword, so it
cannot mint the hybrid on its own.

:END:
*Falsified, and by the docstring of the very function this was about.* The prospective half above
reasons that the fix shape is open, listing "refusing at apply time means blocking a human mid-pass"
as a *cost*. That has the valence backwards. =claude-code-ide-org-set-todo= already documents why it
validates no legality — a queued answer is given against a file that has not moved yet, the
disk-goes-stale problem that retired =bin/hooks/pretooluse-transition-guard= — and names
=org-blocker-hook= as where such a refusal belongs precisely *because* apply runs =org-todo= in front
of a human who can respond to it. A human present at the refusal is the design, not the objection.
So the project had already answered this; the heading's premise that it was open design was wrong,
and it is left standing above rather than edited because the record of the wrong turn is the useful
part.

*Verified, not just implemented:* the guard rests entirely on =org-blocker-hook= being reached for a
keyword **grant** rather than only a transition, which is not obvious and would have failed silently.
Measured in batch before a line was written: the hook is called with =(:from nil :to "TODO")=,
returning nil leaves the keyword unset, returning t sets it. Four assertions then run red first —
refused at child depth *and* at grandchild depth, permitted on the slice's own keyword, permitted on a
note under an ordinary story. 542/542, and since the hook is registered globally every other test in
the suite ran under it too.

Shipped in [[orgit-rev:claude-code-ide-org::dbe2b09][dbe2b09]] as
=claude-code-ide-org--blocker-keyword-inside-slice-p=, beside
=claude-code-ide-org--blocker-clock-running-p= and making the same argument: one function on the hook
covers every path — apply, a hand =C-c C-t=, anything later — without guessing at any of them.

*It denies the grant only*, which is narrower than "no keyworded heading inside a slice" and is the
one design decision made here rather than inherited. A heading that already carries a keyword is
already a hybrid; refusing its later transitions would prevent no minting while wedging the repair,
since it could then never be closed or cancelled on its way out. Clearing a keyword is permitted for
the same reason — that *is* one of the two repairs, the other being a refile out of the slice.

:PROPERTIES:

:CATEGORY: Apply

- [2026-09-03 Thu 13:47]--[2026-09-03 Thu 13:49] assigned at review

Observed 2026-09-03 on :ID: ec65b5d6, the first slice composed and closed within one day. The apply pass landed the members' DONEs and the slice's own DONE in the same ceremony, and refresh-slice then skipped the slice as closed — so it froze at [0/4] with TODO member-line copies and a full :BLOCKER:, a record that was never true at close: every member was already DONE when the slice's transition applied. The skip's rationale (a closed slice is a record, and refreshing lets later work rewrite finished history — :ID: 30a340fd) does not cover same-pass closure, where the refresh would have written exactly the state the close happened under. Fix options: apply refreshes slices after landing member transitions and before landing a slice's own terminal one; or the refresher exempts slices closed by the pass just applied. This instance was hand-derived through Emacs the same day — [4/4], DONE copies, blocker removed.


* TODO The convention checks compare the live Emacs against whichever checkout invoked them :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       f8c86914-0759-4e05-9292-6f7fa5593701
:CREATED:  [2026-09-03 Thu 13:40]
:CATEGORY: Dev
:END:

Run from a git worktree, ~bin/check-conventions~ and ~bin/check-org-dev-skill~ both fail on "claude-code-ide-org-capture-file resolves into this repo". The assertion asks the *running* Emacs where its capture file is and compares that against the root the script was *invoked* from — and the running Emacs serves whichever checkout is symlinked into ~~/.config/doom~, which is never the worktree. Observed 2026-09-03 reviewing PR 8 from ~.claude/worktrees/pr8-review~.

Nothing is wrong with the code the assertion checks. What is wrong is the *cause it reports*: a reader sees a capture-file misconfiguration where the real fact is "you are standing in a different checkout from the one Emacs has loaded" — the same class of defect as :ID: 3f2d5126, arriving one assertion over.

Not urgent, and it does not block commits: the org-dev delegation is gated on ~$docs_only~, which ~.githooks/pre-commit~ always passes, so only a human running the script by hand meets it. Filed because a worktree is now a normal way to review a PR here, so this will recur.

Open, and deliberately not settled here: whether the assertion should detect the worktree and skip with that reason (the ~check_skip~ added in ~590e41c~ is the shape), or whether it should compare against the *common* git directory rather than the invoking root, which would make it pass and keep checking something real.

Found 2026-09-03 when a design revision left :ID: 758a8b78's title stating the withdrawn half of its own proposal. org_amend is body-only, the Edit tool writes behind a live buffer, and the one honest path was a hand emacsclient form — which the read-only buffer then correctly refused, proving CLAUDE.md's point that reaching for emacsclient against a read-only buffer signals a missing tool. * TODO org_query answers from a restricted or short file set without saying so :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       b79b6b78-0bb0-47f2-b84c-a5d969f65252
:CREATED:  [2026-09-03 Thu 09:24]
:END:

Found 2026-09-03 while re-checking whether org_query reaches DONE.org. The tool honors its documented scope — claude-code-ide-org-query-files, falling back to the org-agenda-files function — but that function's answer is shaped by live Emacs state the caller cannot see: an agenda restriction lock (org-agenda-restrict, set by C-x C-< or the dispatcher's < key) silently collapses it to one file, and a dangling path in org-agenda-files (an entry whose symlink was never created) silently contributes nothing. Both produced "No matches" indistinguishable from a true empty result, across three diagnostic layers in one sitting. Proposed: org_query's reply (and ideally org_clock_report's and the recovery scan's) should name the file set it actually searched, and say when a restriction lock is active — "No matches (searched TODO.org; agenda restriction active)" turns a silent wrong answer into an inspectable one.

Three sessions on 2026-09-03 did tracked work — a CLAUDE.md review, then filings and a table-row edit — with no org_clock_in and no org_set_todo, so every span reached review UNASSIGNED. The standing rule triggered only on "asked to start work on a task with an :ID:", every mechanical backstop hung off state transitions, and the "Review and planning" target was advertised only in a schema docstring — the lever :ID: 420816ec measured as the one that does not move usage. Shipped same day (commit e28ea0c): a CLAUDE.md standing rule — before a session's first file-touching act, clock in to the owning heading or to "Review and planning", capturing first if none exists — and bin/hooks/clock-target-check, a Stop hook that blocks once per session when the transcript shows write-tool activity but the session queue holds no clock_in. Clock evidence reads the queue, where errored calls never land; write evidence reads the transcript, which the queue's write-through gate under-reports by design; Edit/Write count only inside the project. Six direct test cases pass. In REVIEW rather than DONE because hook config loads at session start: the first session started after the commit is the live verification.


* TODO A MAYBE member and a dropped member render identically, and the drop is now sticky :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       1b727475-46c3-4967-8cdd-dfd9397d94ea
:CREATED:  [2026-09-02 Wed 18:28]
:CATEGORY: Slices
:END:

*Found 2026-09-02 by reading the result of declaring [[id:ff7ccb2d-2b13-421e-86e3-6b5e187850bf][ff7ccb2d]], minutes after shipping the convention that causes it.*

Two different facts now render as the same line. =MAYBE= and =CANCELLED= map to *no checkbox* in =--slice-checkbox-by-keyword=, deliberately — an unchecked box inflates the denominator forever and reads as a live cookie. And the convention written the same day says a *deleted* cookie means the slice no longer counts that member. So =- [[id:...]] MAYBE Title= is ambiguous between "derived from the keyword" and "dropped by hand", and nothing distinguishes them.

*Visible immediately:* ff7ccb2d reads =[0/2]= over three members, because its =MAYBE= member is uncounted. That number is arguably right — a =MAYBE= member should not be counted, and its exclusion from the =:BLOCKER:= agrees with [[id:72e82469-3d4f-4ee1-b0c4-35b1c253901c][72e82469]], where a =:BLOCKER:= on a =MAYBE= heading is dormant anyway. The rendering is not wrong today; it is *undecidable*.

*The sharp part is that the drop is now sticky, and that is a regression I introduced.* The rewriter computes =dropped= from the *current line* — no cookie means dropped — and then suppresses the box. For a member that is cookie-less only because it is =MAYBE=, promoting it to =TODO= will therefore **never restore its checkbox**: the refresh reads the absent cookie as a declaration and keeps it absent. Before that change the box would have reappeared with the keyword.

*Why it cannot be fixed by reading harder.* At refresh time the only evidence is the current keyword and the current line. A cookie-less line whose referent is now =TODO= is exactly what both cases look like — deliberately dropped, or promoted from =MAYBE= and awaiting its box. The information that distinguishes them was never written down.

*So it needs a distinct marker for "dropped", not a cleverer inference.* Candidates, none chosen: a =[-]= mark, which the member regexp already admits and which org renders as in-progress; wrapping the link in =~...~=; or a property on the slice listing dropped ids. The last is the only one that survives a line being regenerated wholesale, and is also the most machinery.

*Until then the safe operating rule is narrow:* do not promote a =MAYBE= slice member and expect its cookie back. Checked by hand, it returns.

*A concrete instance, 2026-09-03, rather than the hypothetical this was filed on.* =ff7ccb2d= was
expanded from 3 member lines to 20. **Six of them carry no cookie: five are carried =MAYBE=s and one
is a real drop** (=1caed585=). Nothing structural distinguishes them — the drop was a deliberate
decision and the five are ordinary members whose referents merely have not been committed to — and
the only thing telling a reader which is which is a sentence in the slice body, which is exactly the
failure mode this heading names.

*Two things this instance settles that the original filing left open.* The ambiguity is not rare: it
appeared the first time a slice was composed at any real size, at a ratio of 6 lines in 20. And the
disambiguating prose does not scale — one sentence can name one drop among five carries, but it is a
lookup table written in paragraph form, and it will go stale exactly the way the checkbox cannot,
because nothing derives it.

*It also sharpens what a fix would have to do.* Deleting the cookie means "the slice no longer counts
this", and =MAYBE= means "the referent is not committed to". Those are different facts that happen to
render the same, so a fix has to make the *drop* visible rather than make =MAYBE= carry a box — a
box would inflate the denominator forever, which is the reason the mapping exists. A distinct marker
on the dropped line, derived from nothing and set by the drop itself, is the shape that fits.

This project is a testbed for a thing meant to manage *other* projects, and the discipline it has
developed does not leave the directory. The machinery can travel — a plugin ships skills, commands,
hooks and MCP servers — while the prose cannot, because =.claude/rules/= is not a plugin component.
Each member is a different face of that. The argument, the plugin-manifest verification and the
line-count measurement are all =9ae4b17e='s and are not repeated here.

*The order is the point.* =9ae4b17e= classifies what must travel, and every line below assumes its
answer. =d5345abb= then relocates what belongs in the skill and =9d009401= prunes what is left, in
that order, because pruning before the classification risks deleting rules a consuming project needs.
=1ed7b2b4=, =84b7d8b3= and =f8c86914= are the machinery's undeclared assumptions — org version,
interpreter, one-checkout-per-Emacs — each of which fails on a second machine and nowhere here. The
=f90d745e= pair and =83b3cdd6= only mean anything *after* the hooks move, which is why they name
=9ae4b17e= in a =:BLOCKER:= rather than in a sentence. The tail is reader-facing.

*Five cookie-less lines are carried, not dropped* — =e396f94a=, =2e09adb7=, =3cb3f955=, =7eb7dd8d=
and =2c5f7c50=, whose referents are =MAYBE= and so derive no checkbox. A sixth cookie-less line,
=1caed585=, is a real drop. They render identically, which is =1b727475=; until that is settled this
sentence is the only thing telling them apart, so do not read a missing cookie here as a decision.

*=1caed585= is dropped, not pending.* What installation may change on a user's machine is a decision
this slice does not need, and a =MAYBE= in the blocker set would hold the slice open for work it
declined to do.

*=84b7d8b3= was promoted =MAYBE= → =TODO= while this slice was composed*, on merit rather than to
earn it a checkbox: fish stopped being one leaf script and became a prerequisite for committing, via
=bin/check-conventions= and =.githooks/pre-commit=. The other five were considered and held, each
recording on its own heading why — a stale design, a body that argues against its own proposal, an
unresolved open question, or a defect already carried by a sibling member.

Headings that bear on portability but are already declared by =8a2eb687= stay there; membership is
not shared between slices.

* DOING Make the heading body a scannable summary, with plan and debrief in drawers [2/5]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       4c834fdb-5314-4168-ab1d-09fe89ded517
:CREATED:  [2026-09-02 Wed 16:00]
:CATEGORY: Meta
:END:

A heading's body grows without bound, so orientation costs more the longer a task runs — the median live body is 50 lines against a file of ~118,000 tokens. The group's thesis is that the body should be a *fixed-size* artifact: a brief statement of problem and proposal, later a brief resolution, with the reasoning in =:PLAN:= and the outcome in =:DEBRIEF:=. Members cover the two conventions, the two tool changes they need, and the retroactive repair of the corpus written before them.

Precipitated by division 2026-09-02, out of [[id:b75d553a-3c77-4b98-9f58-022bfa295816][b75d553a]], once the fourth member was filed. Its origin findings stay outside the group: [[id:f099379b-7f78-4c19-85be-abeea0bf3e71][f099379b]] measured that the seams are not mechanically recoverable, and [[id:79a3d89e-77be-4678-919e-637a65f87204][79a3d89e]] shipped the nudge that stops new ones being created.


** DONE Write the plan into :PLAN: from the start, so no seam is ever created :code:
CLOSED: [2026-09-02 Wed 16:02]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       b75d553a-3c77-4b98-9f58-022bfa295816
:CREATED:  [2026-09-02 Wed 15:19]
:CATEGORY: Meta
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-09-02 Wed 15:18]--[2026-09-02 Wed 15:20] =>  0:02
- [2026-09-02 Wed 15:18]--[2026-09-02 Wed 15:20] assigned at review
CLOCK: [2026-09-02 Wed 15:44]--[2026-09-02 Wed 15:49] =>  0:05
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-09-02 Wed 15:44] \\
  document the drawer purpose shift
- [2026-09-02 Wed 15:44]--[2026-09-02 Wed 15:49] adjust rules and tool descriptions
CLOCK: [2026-09-02 Wed 15:59]--[2026-09-02 Wed 16:00] =>  0:01
- [2026-09-02 Wed 15:59]--[2026-09-02 Wed 16:00] adjust rules and tool descriptions
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-09-02 Wed 16:02] \\
  convention adopted and documented
:END:
:PLAN:

*The user's proposal, 2026-09-02, offered as a simplification after [[id:f099379b-7f78-4c19-85be-abeea0bf3e71][f099379b]] showed the backlog's seams are not mechanically recoverable:* write the prospective text into =:PLAN:= from the beginning; keep the task body to a brief problem-and-proposed-solution of 2-5 sentences; append the debrief at DONE-queued time.

*It dissolves the seam rather than solving it.* Every mechanism tried so far — =org_wrap_plan='s =until=, the lexical marker, the queued-DONE nudge — exists because prospective and retrospective prose accumulate in one flat body and must be told apart *later*. Writing them to different places at the moment each is composed means there is never a mixture, so nothing has to be separated and no judgement is ever owed. The nudge shipped hours earlier ([[id:79a3d89e-77be-4678-919e-637a65f87204][79a3d89e]]) becomes a *backstop* for headings that did not follow the discipline, rather than the primary mechanism.

*Verified adoptable with today's tools, before proposing anything be built.* Four existing calls produce the shape exactly, measured in a scratch file:

: org_amend <design>  →  org_wrap_plan (no until)  →  org_amend <2-5 sentences>  →  [at DONE] org_amend <debrief>

=org_wrap_plan= with no =until= wraps the whole body, which is precisely right when the body is *still purely prospective* — the case its docstring already names. =org_amend= then appends below the drawer, which =org_wrap_plan='s own tool description already promises. So this needs no new tool, and this heading was written that way.

*The one real tension, and it must be written down or live plans go invisible.* =:PLAN:= currently means *superseded* design that a reader is told to skip — =org_body='s description says so explicitly. Under this convention a *live* heading's drawer holds its **current** plan, which the standing advice would have readers skip exactly when they most need it. The rule has to become: **skip =:PLAN:= on a finished heading, read it on a live one.** That is arguably cleaner than today's, since the drawer's status then follows the heading's keyword rather than being a property of the drawer, but it is a change and not a clarification.

*A second, smaller consequence worth naming.* CLAUDE.md's [[id:b5f94b88-76e3-4a3c-ac73-729f34d33691][b5f94b88]] note says the convention "does not empty the body of a planned heading", citing reasoning and an incident record that live in that body. Those are retrospective, so they stay — but they mean the 2-5 sentence limit governs the body *before* the debrief, not the body forever. Worth stating, because read as an absolute cap it would push debriefs into the drawer, which is the original inversion again.

*The payoff beyond the seam is context cost, and it is large.* CLAUDE.md records the median active heading body at 50 lines. A 2-5 sentence body means =org_outline= and =org_body= reads become cheap for the *live* set, which is the set sessions actually read for orientation. That is the same goal as [[id:9d009401-93dd-40e5-a243-7a30a8a5bd55][9d009401]] approaching from a different direction.

*What is undecided and belongs to the user:* where the rule lives (CLAUDE.md's state-transition section, =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md=, or the org skill), and whether a lint should enforce the cap — a live heading with a body over some threshold and no =:PLAN:= drawer, which is the exact mirror of the finished-heading rule that already exists. Both are cheap; neither should be guessed at.

*Explicitly out of scope:* the 64-heading backlog. This convention stops new seams being created and does nothing for existing ones, which remain [[id:f099379b-7f78-4c19-85be-abeea0bf3e71][f099379b]]'s three routes.
:END:

Prospective and retrospective prose accumulate in one body and have to be told apart later, which [[id:f099379b-7f78-4c19-85be-abeea0bf3e71][f099379b]] measured as not mechanically recoverable — the seam is a fact about when a sentence was written, and nothing in the prose records it. Proposed: write the plan into =:PLAN:= at the moment it is composed, keep the body to a 2-5 sentence problem-and-proposed-solution, and append the debrief when DONE is queued. Then no mixture is ever created and no separation is ever owed. Verified to need no new tools; the open questions are where the rule lives and whether a lint enforces the cap.

*Adopted and documented 2026-09-02.* The convention is stated in all four places it could be contradicted: =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= (canonical section rewritten), =.claude/skills/org/SKILL.md=, CLAUDE.md and its =org_body= tool-table row, and the =org_body= / =org_wrap_plan= descriptions in =config.el=.

*The load-bearing edit was the reading rule, not the writing one.* =:PLAN:= meant "superseded design, skip it". Under plan-first a *live* heading's drawer holds its **current** plan, so the old advice would have hidden every live plan behind an instruction to skip. It now reads: skip on a finished heading, read on a live one — the drawer's status following the heading's keyword rather than being a property of the drawer, which is why nothing has to move when a heading closes.

*Verified adoptable before being written down, and exercised five times since.* =org_amend= → =org_wrap_plan= → =org_amend= produces the shape with no new tooling, measured in a scratch file first. Every heading filed after that point was written this way, including this one.

*Also retired here:* "backlog = wrap unedited", in CLAUDE.md and the skill, with [[id:f099379b-7f78-4c19-85be-abeea0bf3e71][f099379b]]'s measurement as the reason.

*Residue, filed rather than described:* the three-call workflow is clumsy and nothing can revise a drawer once written. Both are [[id:501a8422-9e93-4d2d-98bb-db0ab2c16e37][501a8422]], which would collapse this to one call.


** NEXT Give org_amend a drawer argument, so a plan can be revised and written in one call :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       501a8422-9e93-4d2d-98bb-db0ab2c16e37
:CREATED:  [2026-09-02 Wed 15:59]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-09-02 Wed 16:27] \\
  unblocks the one-call plan-first workflow
:END:
:PLAN:

*The user's proposal, 2026-09-02:* =org_amend= takes a =drawer= argument; null means the body, as today.

*It is a better idea than the gap that prompted it, and this is the argument for it.* The gap was small — nothing can revise a =:PLAN:= drawer, so correcting a plan needs a hand =emacsclient= edit, which [[id:c8a97d9d-8b13-4b6a-a8b9-1a3f24b5e00b][c8a97d9d]] and CLAUDE.md both warn is a signal the tool surface is missing something. But the same parameter **collapses the plan-first workflow from three calls to one**. Today [[id:b75d553a-3c77-4b98-9f58-022bfa295816][b75d553a]] requires =org_amend= → =org_wrap_plan= → =org_amend=; with a drawer argument the first two become =org_amend drawer=PLAN=. A convention that costs one call instead of three is materially more likely to be followed, and this one's whole value is that it is followed every time.

*It also generalises to* [[id:d5eb32a3-fcee-4a25-b39a-fcde597f6cee][d5eb32a3]] *for free.* If the debrief moves into =:DEBRIEF:=, that drawer needs exactly the same write path, and a =drawer= parameter serves it with no further work. A =org_wrap_debrief= sibling would be a second tool doing the first one's job.

*The safety requirement is specific and non-negotiable.* =:PROPERTIES:= and =:LOGBOOK:= are **org-managed** — they hold identity, clock lines and state history, all written by org's own machinery. Appending prose into either would corrupt the record this project exists to keep trustworthy, and would do it silently. The tool must refuse them by name, exactly as =org_set_property= already refuses =:ID:= and =:CREATED:= for the same reason. An allowlist (=PLAN=, =DEBRIEF=) is safer than a denylist, because the next org-managed drawer someone adds is then refused by default rather than accepted by omission.

*Two questions genuinely open.* Whether =drawer=PLAN= should *create* the drawer when absent — which is what makes the one-call workflow possible, and also what makes it partly subsume =org_wrap_plan=. And whether =replace=true= should be allowed against a drawer: it is the only way to revise a plan wholesale, and it is also the most destructive operation in the tool surface pointed at content readers are told to skip on finished headings, so a bad write there is invisible by exactly the argument CLAUDE.md already makes about condensing.

*What it does not replace.* =org_wrap_plan='s =until= seam marker stays the retroactive tool for [[id:35d25265-df52-45c0-8226-dfa24c3ed5bb][35d25265]]'s pass; nothing here helps with a body that already holds both halves.
:END:

Nothing writes into a =:PLAN:= drawer — =org_amend= appends below it — so revising a plan needs a hand =emacsclient= edit, which the conventions treat as a sign the tool surface is short. Proposed: =org_amend= takes a =drawer= argument, null meaning the body as today. That also collapses [[id:b75d553a-3c77-4b98-9f58-022bfa295816][b75d553a]]'s plan-first workflow from three calls to one and serves [[id:d5eb32a3-fcee-4a25-b39a-fcde597f6cee][d5eb32a3]]'s =:DEBRIEF:= drawer with no further work. It must refuse =:PROPERTIES:= and =:LOGBOOK:= by allowlist, since both are org-managed and a silent write there would corrupt the clock and state record.

*The user's proposal, 2026-09-02, alongside [[id:b75d553a-3c77-4b98-9f58-022bfa295816][b75d553a]].* Put the debrief in a =:DEBRIEF:= drawer placed *below* the body, after adding a brief statement of the resolution to the body. Folded, a heading then shows: title, a 2-5 sentence problem-and-proposed-solution, and a one-line resolution — a TL;DR in view while every drawer and property is collapsed.

*What it completes.* [[id:b75d553a-3c77-4b98-9f58-022bfa295816][b75d553a]] moves the prospective half out of the body; this moves the retrospective half out too, leaving the body as *nothing but* the scannable summary. The two together make the body a fixed-size artifact regardless of how much reasoning or evidence a task accumulates — which is the property that makes orientation cheap, and the one neither change achieves alone.

*Ordering, and it is the useful finding here:* this should follow the retroactive separation ([[id:35d25265-df52-45c0-8226-dfa24c3ed5bb][35d25265]]) rather than precede it. Once the plan is in =:PLAN:=, the remaining body *is* the debrief, so wrapping it into =:DEBRIEF:= needs no seam judgement at all. Doing it first would mean deciding two seams instead of one.

*What it costs, stated because it is not free.* The resolution line is *authored*, not derived — a human or a session must write one per heading, and for the 64 retroactive headings that is 64 short summaries. Mechanical wrapping is cheap; the TL;DR is not.

*Open:* whether =org_wrap_plan= generalises to a drawer parameter or a sibling =org_wrap_debrief= is written; whether the lint should require a resolution line once a =:DEBRIEF:= exists; and whether =:DEBRIEF:= should be exempt from the folding advice that tells readers to skip =:PLAN:=, since a debrief on a finished heading is the thing most worth reading.
:END:

A heading's body still grows without bound once the debrief lands in it, so orientation stays expensive even after [[id:b75d553a-3c77-4b98-9f58-022bfa295816][b75d553a]] moves the plan out. Proposed: a =:DEBRIEF:= drawer below the body, with a one-line resolution left in the body — so a folded heading shows problem, proposal and outcome and nothing else. Should follow [[id:35d25265-df52-45c0-8226-dfa24c3ed5bb][35d25265]] rather than precede it, since after the plan is extracted the remaining body *is* the debrief and needs no seam judgement.

In scope, added 2026-09-03: absorb the pre-archive outcome-summary rule (CLAUDE.md near line 505, the summary "next to that link"). Post-b75d553a the link that rule points at sits inside :PLAN:, a drawer readers are told to skip, and once this task splits outcome recording into a body resolution plus a :DEBRIEF: drawer, the :505 rule becomes a third description of the same act, phrased against a location neither artifact will occupy. Rewrite or delete it as part of landing this, rather than patching it now and rewording it twice.

*The user's proposal, 2026-09-02.* Once the plan and (per [[id:d5eb32a3-fcee-4a25-b39a-fcde597f6cee][d5eb32a3]]) the debrief live in drawers, a session reading for orientation should get the *body* by default and reach for drawer content deliberately, per item, when it needs depth. The user's sketch: =org_outline= keeps the body, and =org_body= is refactored — possibly renamed — to serve on demand what =org_outline= does not.

*Why the current split stops fitting.* =org_outline= today returns one line per heading with no body at all, and =org_body= returns a heading whole, drawers included, leaving extraction to the caller. That division made sense when the body *was* the content: outline for structure, body for everything. Under the new shape the body becomes a fixed-size summary, so an outline that omitted it would be withholding the cheapest and most useful thing in the file, while a =org_body= that returns everything is the expensive call it was designed to avoid needing.

*The economy argument is measurable rather than aesthetic.* CLAUDE.md records the median active heading body at 50 lines and TODO.org at ~118,000 tokens. If bodies become 2-5 sentences, an outline carrying them is affordable for the live set and answers most orientation questions outright — which is the job =org_outline= already claims.

*Open, and genuinely undecided:* whether =org_outline= should carry bodies always, only when scoped, or behind a flag; whether the drawer read is a parameter on =org_body= (=drawer:PLAN=) or separate tools; and what =org_body= should be called if its job narrows to "give me the drawer I asked for". Renaming a tool has its own cost — every skill and rule naming it must move together, and [[id:1cd2df62-35e9-47dc-bcce-dda7bae3c522][1cd2df62]] is the standing heading about tool surfaces that lie about what they accept.

*Depends on* [[id:b75d553a-3c77-4b98-9f58-022bfa295816][b75d553a]] and [[id:d5eb32a3-fcee-4a25-b39a-fcde597f6cee][d5eb32a3]]: until bodies are actually short, carrying them in the outline makes it *more* expensive, not less. Sequencing this first would measure the wrong thing.
:END:

Once plan and debrief move into drawers, =org_outline='s bodiless index withholds the cheapest useful content while =org_body='s whole-heading read returns far more than orientation needs. Proposed: =org_outline= carries the (now short) body, and the drawer read becomes an explicit per-item call — a refactored, possibly renamed =org_body=. Depends on [[id:b75d553a-3c77-4b98-9f58-022bfa295816][b75d553a]] and [[id:d5eb32a3-fcee-4a25-b39a-fcde597f6cee][d5eb32a3]]: until bodies are actually short, putting them in the outline makes it more expensive rather than less.
