:PLAN:

Proposed by the user 2026-08-10: a standing pseudo-task to clock against
for grooming, capturing, "what's next" review, and similar activity that
is real work but belongs to no single tracked task. Captured as TODO on
a recommendation, not as a settled decision.

*The concrete argument, from that session's own data.* Of the first ten
events in its queue, *eight* were =pause=/=resume= guideposts that
reduced to nothing at review. Guideposts carry no heading; they inherit
whichever one the surrounding =clock_in=/=clock_out= last named, so
guideposts recorded while no clock is open are unattributable and
silently dropped. Meta-work is precisely when no clock is open — so that
time is not merely misattributed, it is invisible, in direct tension
with this project's second stated goal.

*Check org's own mechanism first, per
[[id:c084553c-0621-4a96-9fa1-f32850aeec6a][c084553c]].* Org already has
a name for the concept: =org-clock-default-task=, set with
=org-clock-mark-default-task=.

*Two risks were recorded, neither disqualifying.* The *dumping-ground*
failure — a large bucket labelled "misc" answers no question worth
asking, so it must be scoped to genuinely cross-cutting work. And it
*never reaches DONE*: a permanently open task sits among siblings
governed by the single-=NEXT= triggers, and it never archives, so its
=:LOGBOOK:= grows without bound. Probably fine for a pure time record,
but a decision rather than an oversight.

*Refined 2026-08-10, and the refinement narrows what this is for.* The
user proposed the general principle: grooming, defining, amending and
adjusting a future task is *activity on that task*, and should be
clocked against it, leaving its TODO state untouched. Never stated
anywhere, though nothing forbade it — CLAUDE.md's rules say a transition
*to* =DOING= opens a clock and one *from* it closes one, never the
converse, so clocking a =TODO= heading was always legal by the letter
and simply undescribed. Confirmed safe the same day:
=org-clock-in-switch-to-state= is nil here, and this heading's own
editorial session was clocked against
[[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]] while that heading
stayed literally =TODO=.

Attribution is then exact where it applies — time spent working out what
a task should be lands on that task, next to the reasoning it produced.
A catch-all could answer "how much meta-work happened" but never "which
task was I circling." *So the two are complementary and this heading
shrinks to the residue:* work belonging to no single task — backlog-wide
"what's next?" surveys, cross-cutting review, triage before a task
exists to attach to. Smaller than the original framing assumed, which
also blunts the dumping-ground risk: anything reaching the catch-all is
there because it truly has no home.

*Consequence accepted deliberately:* a =TODO= heading can accrue real
clock time without ever having been =DOING=. Correct — deciding what a
task is *is* work — but "time spent" and "was worked on" stop being the
same question, and any future reporting must keep them apart.

*Alternative captured 2026-08-11, narrowing this further:*
[[id:3d0487f4-b8f8-451c-88bf-ab537663cbd0][3d0487f4]] proposes that the
review buffer surface unattributed guideposts for retrospective
assignment, rather than requiring anyone to clock into a standing task
in advance. If that lands, this stops being a mechanism to remember and
becomes a convenient assignment target.

:END:
*Decided 2026-08-21, in conversation with the user, and scoped into five children.*

The standing pseudo-task becomes a *datetree*: one node per day under a
=Review and planning= category, clocked against directly rather than
captured beneath.

*The day node answers this heading's own unbounded-=:LOGBOOK:= risk* by
construction — it is bounded, and the year rolls into DONE.org on the
existing =:ARCHIVE:= route, so the unbounded case never arises.

*The dumping-ground risk gets one concrete guard:* the day node carries
*no TODO keyword*, so =claude-code-ide-org--review-suggest-heading= will
never propose it, and after
[[id:62c6b1be-4e3c-4ea1-9f0c-19ee70be0e1e][62c6b1be]] it would refuse a
grouping anyway. Every span landing there costs a deliberate =a= at
review: a place time is *assigned* to, not one it *falls* into.

*Creation is on demand, not scheduled.* The user asked whether a cron
job or a recurrence should create the node; measured against three
consequences — empty nodes on non-working days, a stored id that can go
stale, and a scheduler failure that announces nothing — the answer was
neither. See [[id:9575e65b-b589-465a-830c-286541780f96][9575e65b]]. The
daily =SCHEDULED: <... +1d>= repeater is kept for the *ritual*: it
reminds, it does not create.

*One draft was falsified along the way*
([[id:25ec37b0-460f-404d-be83-8a4b4d355cdc][25ec37b0]]): the outline
path in =file+olp+datetree= does not nest the tree — nesting comes from
a =:DATE_TREE:= property. Worth knowing before writing the capture
template from the template name alone, which is how the first draft went
wrong.

*Correction [2026-08-27 Thu]: a paragraph claiming =org-clock-default-task= was "adopted … now wired rather than merely noted" was wrong,* and contradicted its own next sentence, which said Claude cannot use it at all because every clock call goes through =org_clock_in= with an =:ID:=. [[id:6ae83993-906c-4d8b-b11e-c91be8996962][6ae83993]] states the fact: *zero references to =clock-default-task= across =modules/= and the Doom config.* It has never been wired.

*What actually shipped is ours, not org's:* =claude-code-ide-org--ensure-day-node= plus =org_clock_in= accepting the *category title* in place of an =:ID:=, resolving today's node on demand and filing the interval against it. That is why allocation works today and has for days — the user noticed exactly this and asked.

*So the check-org-first item was closed against the wrong evidence.* Org did have a name for the concept, which is what the item asked. It does not follow that we used it, and the body recorded the first as though it settled the second. Being right about the search and wrong about the outcome is the more dangerous shape, because it reads as diligence.

*What =org-clock-default-task= would still buy, narrowly:* the *human*, in Emacs, clocking meta-work with =C-u C-u C-c C-x C-i= without navigating to today's node. Nothing for Claude, nothing for the queue. 6ae83993 stays open on those terms. And the interval it would produce is inactive, necessarily — measured 2026-08-27, =org-clock-in= writes =CLOCK: [ts]--[ts]= and org has no active variant of a CLOCK line at all; the distinction this project cares about lives on the *annotation* line beneath, so what marks such an interval as the human's own is the annotation's *absence* ([[id:4f8500e6-b468-4fd4-a0f6-ebf04c7a57ba][4f8500e6]]).

*Two further corrections, 2026-09-01, both found by checking rather than recalling.*

- The never-reaches-DONE risk above named =--trigger-auto-promote-sole-todo= as one of the triggers it would sit among. That trigger was *retired* — 163 lines removed, along with its three guards — and only test comments still mention it. =--trigger-demote-conflicting-next= survives and is the only one left that a permanently open sibling could interact with.
- *The rule this heading asked for was never written.* It called for a line in CLAUDE.md under "State transition rules" stating that a clock may run on a heading in any state, and that grooming attaches to the task being groomed. Neither CLAUDE.md nor =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= says so today. The convention is in force and undocumented, which is the condition this heading was raised to end.


** TODO Teach org_capture to target a datetree, with an addressable day node :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       25ec37b0-460f-404d-be83-8a4b4d355cdc
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 09:51]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-21 Fri 09:52] \\
  datetree target and addressable day node
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-21 Fri 10:27] \\
  Auto-promoted: sole remaining TODO in its sibling group.
- State "TODO"       from "NEXT"       [2026-08-21 Fri 10:47] \\
  Auto-demoted: superseded by sibling [[id:e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd][bin/lint-org will drown in datetree scaffolding it can never satisfy]] becoming NEXT.
:END:

=claude-code-ide-org--capture-target-spec= resolves exactly three shapes: =(file F)=, =(id ID)= and =(file+olp F CATEGORY)=. A datetree needs a fourth.

*The olp in =file+olp+datetree= does not nest the tree, which is the opposite of what the template's own name suggests.* Read from org-capture.el rather than assumed: the branch resolves the outline path to a marker, goes to it -- and then calls =(widen)= and hands =org-datetree-find-date-create= the date with *no* =keep-restriction= argument. The marker is discarded. Nesting comes from somewhere else entirely.

*Two mechanisms actually control placement*, both in =org-datetree--find-create-group=:

- =keep-restriction= = ='subtree-at-point=, which narrows to the heading at point and sets =org-datetree-base-level= from it. Capture never passes this.
- a =:DATE_TREE:= property, found anywhere in the file by =org-find-property= after the widen. When present, org narrows to that heading and sets the base level from it. *This is the one capture actually uses.*

*Materialized both ways rather than reasoned about.* Without the property, two captures against =* Review and planning= produced two separate =* 2026= trees at *level 1*, outside the category and duplicated. With =:DATE_TREE: t= on the category, one tree nested correctly:

#+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
,* Review and planning
:PROPERTIES:
:DATE_TREE: t
:ARCHIVE:  DONE.org::* Review and planning
:END:
,** 2026
,*** 2026-08 August
,**** 2026-08-19 Wednesday
,**** 2026-08-21 Friday
#+END_EXAMPLE

*=:DATE_TREE:= is permitted on a category by the narrowed rule, not in spite of it.* =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= allows "a *structural* property, which says something about the grouping rather than about work", and names =:ARCHIVE:= as the case that forced the wording. =:DATE_TREE:= is the same kind of statement -- it says how this grouping is organised -- and sits beside =:ARCHIVE:= on identical footing.

*The day node is bare, and that is the part that blocks everything else.* Org writes =**** 2026-08-21 Friday= with no property drawer at all. The whole point of this design is to *clock against the day node*, and every tool here addresses headings by =:ID:=, so the capture path must stamp =:ID:= and =:CREATED:= on the day node at creation. Without that the node exists and is unreachable.

*Verified on org 9.6.15* (the batch Emacs =bin/test= runs). The live Emacs is on *9.8.7*; both carry =org-datetree--find-create-group= and the =:DATE_TREE:= lookup, but the split is [[id:1ed7b2b4-a809-4547-b04f-099a3111fc1e][1ed7b2b4]]'s undeclared-dependency gap showing up in practice, and a datetree test will run against the older one.


** MAYBE Point org-clock-default-task at the day node, and re-point it daily :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       6ae83993-906c-4d8b-b11e-c91be8996962
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 09:51]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-21 Fri 09:52] \\
  wire and re-point the default task
- State "MAYBE"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-28 Fri 07:55] \\
  buys only human convenience now
:END:

=org-clock-default-task= is org's own answer to "somewhere to clock cross-cutting time", which is why [[id:a1d63f52-4c07-4b9e-8f31-6d2b90c4e7a5][a1d63f52]] pointed at it under c084553c's check-org-first rule. Chosen by the user 2026-08-21, alongside the datetree.

*It is entirely unwired today.* Zero references to =clock-default-task= across =modules/= and =~/.config/doom/config.el=. Nothing to migrate; this is a first wiring.

*It moves every day, and nothing moves it.* The variable holds a *marker*, set from point by =org-clock-mark-default-task=. With a datetree the intended target is a different heading each morning, so on any day after the first the default task points at yesterday. Something has to re-point it -- a =SessionStart= hook, an idle timer, or on-demand resolution when the day node is created. Deciding which is the substance of this heading; the wiring itself is one =setq=.

*It serves the human and not Claude, and that asymmetry should be accepted deliberately rather than discovered.* =org-clock-default-task= is a live-Emacs convenience: clock into it with a keystroke. Claude cannot use it at all -- every clock call here goes through =org_clock_in= with an =:ID:=, and the queue architecture means Claude never touches a live clock in the first place. So this buys *you* one-keystroke meta-work clocking, while Claude still needs the day node resolved to a UUID.

*Do not solve Claude's half here.* Resolving "today's node" to an =:ID:= without pasting a UUID is [[id:915378ac-3bd4-461d-b0f0-83f67fbb3dac][915378ac]]'s subject, under [[id:478d6ec9-1b99-4c6c-88e9-c793ad4b2f22][478d6ec9]]. Building a second lookup here would be the parallel-convention mistake c084553c exists to prevent, one level down.


** DONE bin/lint-org will drown in datetree scaffolding it can never satisfy :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-21 Fri 13:20]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 09:51]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-21 Fri 09:52] \\
  exempt datetree scaffolding from CREATED
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-21 Fri 10:32] \\
  must land before the tree exists
CLOCK: [2026-08-21 Fri 11:59]--[2026-08-21 Fri 12:00] =>  0:01
- [2026-08-21 Fri 11:59]--[2026-08-21 Fri 12:00] measure the real lint scope
CLOCK: [2026-08-21 Fri 13:16]--[2026-08-21 Fri 13:20] =>  0:04
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-08-21 Fri 13:16] \\
  revise the level rule for the datetree
- [2026-08-21 Fri 13:16]--[2026-08-21 Fri 13:20] teach the lint about datetree scaffolding
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-21 Fri 13:20] \\
  level rule scoped, four mutations verified
:END:
:PLAN:

=bin/lint-org= warns on any heading missing =:CREATED:=. Datetree scaffolding can never satisfy it: org writes =** 2026=, =*** 2026-08 August= and =**** 2026-08-21 Friday= bare, and a year node has no creation moment worth stamping anyway.

*The arithmetic is what makes this a blocker rather than a nuisance.* One day node per working day plus a month node each month is on the order of 300-400 headings a year, against *18 warnings in the whole repo today*. The scaffolding would outnumber the real findings roughly twenty to one inside the first year, and a warning list nobody reads is the same as no lint at all -- the failure mode [[id:e0904e93-b99d-44ad-b641-e3e13aced8c4][e0904e93]] just finished fixing in the session-start report, arriving in a second place.

*Fix it before the tree exists, not after.* Once several hundred warnings are already there the temptation is to silence the check rather than exempt the class, and those are very different decisions.

*Exempt the class, not the file.* The day node is the one that will carry =:ID:= and =:CREATED:= (see [[id:25ec37b0-460f-404d-be83-8a4b4d355cdc][25ec37b0]]), so it should stay linted like any other tracked heading; only the *year and month* nodes are pure structure. That distinction is mechanical -- a datetree year or month node is a heading whose title matches org's own datetree format and which sits under a =:DATE_TREE:= heading -- so the exemption can be stated precisely rather than as "skip anything that looks like a date".

*Note this is the same shape as the categories exemption already in the linter*, which skips =:ID:=/=:CREATED:= on top-level headings because they are structure rather than work. Worth reading that code first; this may be one more case for an existing predicate rather than a new branch.

*Measured 2026-08-21 by linting the exact proposed structure, and the scope above is understated.* The warning flood is the least of it — the real blockers are three *errors*, which the pre-commit hook refuses on:

#+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
error: heading has no :ID:: 2026
error: heading has no :ID:: 2026-08 August
error: level-4 heading; the file has three levels (category, task, epic-child): 2026-08-21 Friday
warn : heading has no :CREATED:: 2026
warn : heading has no :CREATED:: 2026-08 August
#+END_EXAMPLE

Two corrections follow. The year and month nodes fail on *=:ID:=*, an error, before they ever reach the =:CREATED:= warning this heading was written about. And the *level-4 rule rejects the day node itself* — the one heading that does carry =:ID:= and =:CREATED:=, and the one we most want linted. Exempting scaffolding does not reach it.

*The level rule is an assertion with a source*, recorded on [[id:3bd3402b-3ab6-4ace-b20b-3ff84caa3fb2][3bd3402b]] and restated in =bin/lint-org='s header as "the file has three levels (category, task, epic-child)". A datetree makes that false: category, year, month, day is four before any task exists. So this is not a lint tweak but a revision of a documented structural claim about TODO.org, and 3bd3402b should be read before the rule is touched.

*Consequence for sequencing:* proceeding as previously scoped would exempt the two warnings, then hit three errors at [[id:cd1e974e-e006-4974-b770-cdc1cf2e4ab2][cd1e974e]] with the category half-written.

:END:
*Shipped 2026-08-21, before the tree exists.* All three errors are gone and none of them was silenced. =claude-code-ide-org--lint-file= now tracks the innermost enclosing =:DATE_TREE:= heading's level while it maps entries -- =org-map-entries= walks in document order, so an anchor is always seen before its descendants -- and classifies each descendant by a new =claude-code-ide-org--datetree-node-role=.

*The level rule is scoped, not dropped.* 3bd3402b's claim -- 624 headings, zero at level 4 -- was true of a file with no datetree in it. It now reads: three levels outside a =:DATE_TREE:= subtree, and inside one exactly org's year/month scaffolding plus its day node. A level-4 heading anywhere else is still an error, and so is anything *below* a day node, since nothing is filed under one. =bin/lint-org='s header carries the same revised wording; =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= never stated the rule, so nothing there needed changing.

*Gated on org's literal title shapes, not on depth*, and that is the load-bearing choice. A =:DATE_TREE:= category holds real tasks beside its tree -- [[id:cd1e974e-e006-4974-b770-cdc1cf2e4ab2][cd1e974e]]'s ritual repeater is a level-2 heading under that very category, exactly where the year node sits -- so a depth-only exemption would have waived =:ID:= and =:CREATED:= for it and for anything ever refiled there. That is the same over-application shape guarded twice in [[id:42808717-2736-4bf4-872e-887f990203ca][42808717]] and [[id:c8a6c5d2-8777-44d2-9900-e56eb03aa2d9][c8a6c5d2]] earlier the same day: a predicate reading position without asking what kind of heading it is looking at. The patterns are org's own =comparefun= regexes, read out of the straight checkout's =org-datetree.el= rather than recalled. Quarter and ISO-week groupings are deliberately not accepted.

*The day node stays linted, which took an explicit fall-through.* Writing the level clause as a branch that matched the day node and did nothing would have exempted the one heading here that most needs =:ID:= -- it is what this project clocks against. The condition is =and=-ed instead, so a day node falls through to the ordinary =:ID:=/=:CREATED:= checks.

*Verified by mutation, four ways, each failing exactly its own test:* removing the year/month exemption, refusing the day node its level-4 allowance, making the role depth-only, and treating the day node as scaffolding. The third is the one that matters -- it is the only mutation the depth-only design would have shipped. Fixtures are literal text and never call =org-datetree-find-date-create=, keeping [[id:1ed7b2b4-a809-4547-b04f-099a3111fc1e][1ed7b2b4]]'s 9.6-vs-9.8 org split out of a lint that only reads structure. =bin/lint-org= on the real files: 0 errors, 23 pre-existing warnings.

*A second datetree is already visible, and this rule does not cover it.* Measured 2026-08-21, hours after shipping: if DONE.org ever takes an =org-archive-location= of the =datetree/= form, org builds a year/month/day tree there *with no =:DATE_TREE:= property on it* and files entries at *level 4* beneath the day node. Against the rule as written that is four errors per archived item -- three scaffolding nodes with no =:ID:=, plus a level-4 heading that is not a day node.

Not a defect in what shipped: the rule was scoped to the structure that existed, and it correctly refuses a shape nobody has adopted. But it means the exemption is keyed on a property org's own archive path never writes, so "recognise a datetree" and "recognise our =:DATE_TREE:= anchor" are not the same predicate. Whoever takes up [[id:29439196-bbb8-4b64-b8d5-3bf9d457bf6c][29439196]] or the archive-cadence question should read this rule before choosing an archive location, not after.


** DONE Resolve today's node on demand, never storing or scheduling its ID :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-24 Mon 12:31]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       9575e65b-b589-465a-830c-286541780f96
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 10:10]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-21 Fri 10:11] \\
  build the on-demand resolver
- State "NEXT"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-21 Fri 13:22] \\
  nominated: resolver gates both remaining siblings
CLOCK: [2026-08-24 Mon 12:27]--[2026-08-24 Mon 12:31] =>  0:04
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-08-24 Mon 12:27] \\
  build the on-demand day-node resolver
- [2026-08-24 Mon 12:27]--[2026-08-24 Mon 12:31] build the day-node resolver
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-24 Mon 12:31] \\
  resolver shipped, dated from the event
CLOCK: [2026-08-24 Mon 12:46]--[2026-08-24 Mon 12:48] =>  0:02
- [2026-08-24 Mon 12:46]--[2026-08-24 Mon 12:48] make the category assignable at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-24 Mon 13:02]--[2026-08-24 Mon 13:03] =>  0:01
- [2026-08-24 Mon 13:02]--[2026-08-24 Mon 13:03] make the category assignable at review
:END:
:PLAN:

One function answers "which heading is today's meta-work node", and it is called at the moment something needs the answer rather than run ahead of time. Steps: find the =:DATE_TREE:= heading; =org-datetree-find-date-create= for today, which is org's own idempotent find-or-create; stamp =:ID:= and =:CREATED:= if the node was just made; set =org-clock-default-task= to its marker; return the id.

*Two callers, and the asymmetry between them is the design.* =org_clock_in= calls it when the target names the category, and *creates* if absent. The =SessionStart= hook calls it to refresh =org-clock-default-task= and must *never* create -- a session starting is not evidence that meta-work happened.

*Why on demand rather than scheduled, which was the question that produced this heading.* The user asked 2026-08-21 whether creation should be a cron job or a recurrence. Three consequences decided it:

- *A schedule manufactures empty nodes.* One per calendar day whether or not anything happened -- on the order of 365 a year against a working year's worth of real ones. That is [[id:e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd][e30d52d7]]'s scaffolding problem being fed rather than contained.
- *A stored id goes stale; a computed one cannot.* The premise of the original question -- "the default id would have to be updated to match today's node" -- is only true if something remembers yesterday's. Recomputing deletes the failure mode instead of scheduling around it.
- *Nothing announces a scheduler that stopped running.* A missing node is noticed the moment someone clocks; a silent scheduler failure is noticed at review, days later, as absent time.

*The scheduling tools available to Claude cannot do this job anyway.* =CronCreate= and the =schedule= skill run *cloud* agents, which have no route to this machine's Emacs server. Recorded from the tooling description rather than from a test, but the shape is not marginal. A local scheduler would also fire on non-working days, and -- recursively -- a session spawned to fix attribution generates its own guideposts needing attribution.

*Claude never learns the daily UUID, which is the point.* The target is the stable category title =Review and planning=, reusing the precedent =claude-code-ide-org--capture-target-spec= already argues for: top-level categories are few, human-curated, and never move, so the properties that make titles unsafe elsewhere do not hold. That sidesteps [[id:915378ac-3bd4-461d-b0f0-83f67fbb3dac][915378ac]] rather than waiting on it.

*Shared by [[id:25ec37b0-460f-404d-be83-8a4b4d355cdc][25ec37b0]] and [[id:6ae83993-906c-4d8b-b11e-c91be8996962][6ae83993]]*, which each need part of it -- the day node's =:ID:= and the default-task marker respectively. Build it once here rather than twice there.

*Open design question, recorded 2026-08-21 (user-flagged for their decision, found reviewing the next-session prompt).* "Two callers: =org_clock_in= creates" is unsettled, not settled: =org_clock_in= is a queued tool that by architecture changes nothing when called. Either (a) the resolver runs at *queue time* -- an immediate file write triggered by a queued tool, a new hybrid class like =org_amend=, so the day-node =:ID:= is resolved while "today" is still today; or (b) it runs at *apply time* inside the review command -- pure, but an event queued late Monday and applied Tuesday resolves "today" to the wrong day's node. (a) keeps the timestamp honest and (b) keeps the tool pure; they cannot both hold. Decide before building; the next-session prompt no longer implies this is specified.

*The open question above has three answers, not the two it poses.* Recorded 2026-08-21 at the user's request, so the analysis does not have to be rebuilt; the decision itself is still theirs.

*(a) Queue time -- =org_clock_in= creates the node when called.* Resolves "today" while today is still today, and hands Claude the id inside the session. Against: it breaks the invariant that queued tools change nothing, which was bought with a documented run of desync bugs; a background or concurrent session would write to TODO.org unreviewed; and a node is created even for a clock event the human later dismisses, which is precisely the empty-node problem this heading's on-demand design exists to prevent.

*(b) Apply time, dated "today" -- the review command creates it.* Tools stay pure, every write stays human-triggered, and no node is made for dismissed work. Against, and disqualifying on its own: an event queued Monday 23:00 and applied Tuesday files Monday's work under Tuesday. Silent wrong attribution is the failure this whole project exists to prevent.

*(c) Apply time, dated from the event's own timestamp.* Everything (b) has, with (b)'s only real defect removed -- using data the event already carries, since every queued event is stamped when it is written. Against: Claude never learns the day-node id during the session.

*Recommended: (c), because its single cost is one this heading has already accepted.* The paragraph above says so outright -- "Claude never learns the daily UUID, which is the point", the target being the stable category title =Review and planning=. So (c) is not a compromise between the other two. It strictly dominates (b), and beats (a) without surrendering anything the design wanted.

*Two consequences to know before committing.* =org-clock-default-task= degrades gracefully rather than silently: the =SessionStart= hook still resolves today's node read-only and never creates, so before the first apply the default task is simply unset -- [[id:6ae83993-906c-4d8b-b11e-c91be8996962][6ae83993]]'s territory, already off the critical path. And a late apply becomes *correct* rather than convenient: meta-work clocked Monday and applied Friday creates Monday's node on Friday, dated Monday. That reads odd until it is set beside the archive behaviour measured the same day on [[id:29439196-bbb8-4b64-b8d5-3bf9d457bf6c][29439196]] -- =org-archive-subtree= files by =CLOSED=, not by archive time. "File by when it happened, not by when it was recorded" is then one principle holding in two places, rather than a special case invented here.

*DECIDED 2026-08-21 by the user: option (c).* The resolver runs at *apply time* and dates the node from *the event's own timestamp*, never from "today".

This supersedes the "Two callers, and the asymmetry between them is the design" paragraph above, which says "=org_clock_in= calls it when the target names the category, and *creates* if absent". It does not create. =org_clock_in= queues an event as every queued tool does, and creation happens when that event is applied, against the day the event was stamped. The =SessionStart= half of that paragraph is unchanged and still correct: it resolves read-only and never creates.

*What to build, restated under the decision:* find the =:DATE_TREE:= heading; =org-datetree-find-date-create= for *the event's date*; stamp =:ID:= and =:CREATED:= if the node was just made; set =org-clock-default-task=; return the id. The only change from the original steps is the date argument, which is why this is a smaller build than the open question made it look.

/Recorded by appending, which is itself/ [[id:3063c3e5-0dd0-4354-8985-271b0313c5d1][3063c3e5]]/'s defect: the decision sits below the superseded paragraph rather than replacing it, so a reader meets the stale version first./

:END:
*Shipped 2026-08-24* (=2b9be1c=), built to the decision recorded above: option (c), apply time, dated from the event.

=claude-code-ide-org-resolve-day-node= takes a TIME and an optional CREATE. =org_clock_in= now accepts the category title =Review and planning= as its target and *creates nothing* -- queuing stays a line appended to a file. =--resolve-item-target= turns that target into a real day node at apply, dated from the item's own timestamp.

*Verified live against the real TODO.org, read-only:* the category is recognised; today resolves to =nil= because no node exists and none was made; 2026-08-21 resolves to =758643f2=, the node that actually exists; and =git status= stayed clean throughout, so read-only really is read-only. That last check matters more than it looks -- the =SessionStart= caller depends on it, and a resolver that quietly created would manufacture exactly the one-node-per-calendar-day problem this heading's design exists to prevent.

*The dating is pinned by a test that fails under the rejected design.* Reintroducing option (b) -- =(current-time)= instead of the event's timestamp -- fails =test-apply-resolves-a-category-target= and nothing else. The fixture asks for a node dated 2026-08-17 and asserts both that org wrote =2026-08-17 Monday= and that nothing dated today appeared, so it cannot pass by accident on a day that happens to match.

*=:CREATED:= is stamped from the event as well*, not from now. A node minted on Friday for Monday's work was created, as a record, on Monday; stamping it Friday would put a date in the drawer that contradicts the heading title directly above it.

*Nesting is load-bearing and is asserted.* =org-narrow-to-subtree= plus =find-date-create='s =keep-restriction= keeps the tree inside the category. Without it org writes a second =* 2026= at level 1, which =bin/lint-org= would then flag as an uncategorised level-1 heading -- the failure [[id:95087d8f-e16d-45d3-bdea-9b179dc672b4][95087d8f]] filed after =* *= swallowed 6462 lines.

*Not built here, and deliberately:* the =SessionStart= refresh of =org-clock-default-task=. The read-only half of the resolver is what that needs and it exists; wiring it is [[id:6ae83993-906c-4d8b-b11e-c91be8996962][6ae83993]], which this heading always said was off the critical path. Until then the default task is simply unset, which is the graceful degradation the decision anticipated.

*Now unblocked:* the meta-work spans sitting unassigned in the queue from 08-17, 08-22 and today can be assigned to the category and will file themselves under the right day. That was the practical reason this went first.


** DONE Institute the Review and planning category, with its DATE_TREE and ritual repeater :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-21 Fri 13:21]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       cd1e974e-e006-4974-b770-cdc1cf2e4ab2
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 10:10]
:BLOCKER:  ids(e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd)
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-21 Fri 10:11] \\
  institute the category once lint is exempt
CLOCK: [2026-08-21 Fri 13:20]--[2026-08-21 Fri 13:21] =>  0:01
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-21 Fri 13:20] \\
  institute the Review and planning category
- [2026-08-21 Fri 13:20]--[2026-08-21 Fri 13:21] write the category, repeater and archive mirror
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-21 Fri 13:21] \\
  category instituted, lint and placement verified
:END:
:PLAN:

The act of putting the design into TODO.org. Deliberately last: the user's instruction 2026-08-21 was that the branch and the real edit belong together, at the moment the structure lands rather than while it is being scoped.

*The whole initial subtree, as org actually writes it* -- materialized, not sketched, and comma-escaped per [[id:8a23d6ec-ca45-454d-b65f-e46ea9204816][8a23d6ec]]:

The shape it created is documented in =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= under "The meta-work datetree" -- moved there 2026-08-24 because it is a standing structural fact rather than this task's history, and because the =:PROPERTIES:=/=:END:= pairs in the worked example made this body unwrappable: =:END:= is org's drawer terminator and closes a =:PLAN:= drawer wherever it appears.

A matching =* Review and planning= must be added to DONE.org in the same edit, or the =:ARCHIVE:= target is matched as a literal string, fails to resolve, and org silently appends a second near-identical category at the end of the file.

*The repeater reminds; it does not create.* Chosen by the user 2026-08-21 alongside the on-demand resolver, and the division is load-bearing: creation is [[id:9575e65b-b589-465a-830c-286541780f96][9575e65b]]'s job and happens when work happens. If the repeater created the node instead, every calendar day would produce one whether or not anything occurred, and the id would be re-coupled to a schedule -- both of the things the resolver exists to avoid. What the repeater buys is the ritual: a standing daily prompt to actually do review and planning.

*Verified that the repeater and the datetree coexist*, which was not obvious -- the repeater sits at =org-datetree-base-level=, the same level org wants for year nodes. Materialized with two dates months apart: org skipped the non-year sibling, inserted =** 2026= after it, and kept month order (August before December). No clash.

*A category is the one place in this repo a repeater is safe.* [[id:96a51c2f-ce11-4b40-bdff-11223d3d4eb7][96a51c2f]] records the trap: a repeater never reaches DONE, so it has no completable parent, and being one made an epic uncompletable. A category carries no keyword and completes nothing, so the trap does not apply.

*Blocked on [[id:e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd][e30d52d7]] and not on the others.* That heading's own instruction is to fix the linter "before the tree exists, not after", because once several hundred warnings are present the temptation is to silence the check rather than exempt the class. The datetree tooling can land in either order; the linter cannot.

:END:
*Shipped 2026-08-21*, as one edit across both files: the category with its =:DATE_TREE:= and =:ARCHIVE:=, the ritual repeater, today's day node, and the matching bare =* Review and planning= at the end of DONE.org. Placed last among the categories in both files. Written exactly as materialized above, with one addition -- today's day node (=2026-08-21 Friday=, [[id:758643f2-2046-4894-8a25-7ab44e4dad9a][758643f2]]) was hand-created with =:ID:= and =:CREATED:=, so meta-work can be clocked today rather than waiting on [[id:9575e65b-b589-465a-830c-286541780f96][9575e65b]].

*The DONE.org mirror carries no =:DATE_TREE:=, deliberately.* Archiving moves a subtree under the target heading as an ordinary child; nothing runs a datetree capture against DONE.org, so the property would assert a structure that file does not have. An archived day node lints there as an ordinary level-2 heading, since it keeps the =:ID:= and =:CREATED:= it was created with.

*=bin/lint-org= on the real files with the tree present: 0 errors, 23 warnings* -- the same 23 as before it existed. That is [[id:e30d52d7-960b-44f3-ab92-93846c81a1bd][e30d52d7]]'s fix verified against the real structure rather than against fixtures, which is the check that could only be run at this moment.

*Placement verified against the real file, not a sketch.* A copy was driven through =org-datetree-find-date-create= for two dates months apart. Both landed *inside* the category: =2026-08-22 Saturday= nested under the existing =2026-08 August= in date order, and a new =2026-12 December= after it in month order. The repeater sits at =org-datetree-base-level=, the same level org wants for year nodes, and org skipped it rather than clashing with it.

*One consequence worth knowing before 9575e65b lands.* Org writes day nodes bare, so any node org creates that the resolver has not stamped will fail the lint with =heading has no :ID:=. That is the intended guard, not a gap: a day node without an =:ID:= exists and is unreachable, and the lint now says so loudly instead of the node being silently unusable.


* TODO Stale entries in org-clock's persisted history hang the first clock-in after a restart :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Clock
:ID:       001fb310-69f7-4417-81be-a224a3bf83e0
:CREATED:  [2026-08-10 Mon 11:51]
:END:

Hit live 2026-08-10 while setting =f9f61c04= to =DOING=. The
=org_set_todo= MCP call timed out, while Emacs itself stayed responsive
to =emacsclient -e= -- a recursive edit still runs a command loop, so
the server keeps answering. That combination makes a blocked Emacs look
like a tool fault.

*Mechanism, fully root-caused:* Doom advises =org-clock-in= with
=+org--clock-load-a=, a =:before= advice that lazily calls
=org-clock-load= on the *first* clock-in of an Emacs session.
=org-clock-load= visits every file named in the persisted history
(=org-clock-persist-file=, here
=~/.config/emacs/.local/etc/org-clock-save.el=). That history still
named =/private/tmp/planning-verify/scratch.org=, a scratch file from an
earlier verification session whose directory /tmp reaping had since
removed. Visiting it produced a buffer whose save asked
=Directory .../planning-verify/ does not exist! Create it? (y or n)= --
and a =y-or-n-p= is *not* an error, so no =condition-case= in this
module could have caught it. It simply waited.

*Why it first looked like leaked state:* a separate =emacsclient= query
reported =claude-code-ide-org--auto-clock-in-active= as =t=, which reads
exactly like a re-entrancy guard that failed to unwind. It was not --
the =let=-binding was still live on the blocked call's stack. Reading it
that way is what located the hang inside =--trigger-auto-clock-in=, and
it generalizes: a dynamic binding observed as set from outside means the
frame that bound it has not returned.

*Cleared 2026-08-10*: =abort-recursive-edit=, killed the stale buffer,
filtered =org-clock-history= of markers into nonexistent files, and
called =org-clock-save= to rewrite the persist file from the cleaned
live history. Verified -- the file no longer mentions the path, and the
next =org_clock_in= returned immediately.

*What is NOT fixed, and is the actual task here:* nothing prevents a
recurrence. Any verification pass that clocks into a scratch org file
under /tmp writes that path into the persisted history, and the first
clock-in after the next restart hangs once /tmp is reaped. =bin/test=
runs =emacs --batch -Q= and cannot pollute the real history, but hand
verification in a live Emacs does exactly this -- and
[[id:3d576d29-747f-40b9-a5b3-ecc76679daad][3d576d29]] established that
live verification is *necessary* here, because batch behaves
differently. Options, unevaluated: prune nonexistent files via an
=org-clock-load= advice; keep live-verification scratch files somewhere
/tmp reaping does not reach; or set =org-clock-persist= to nil and
accept losing clock history across restarts.

Relevant to [[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]]: a
hang in the clock path is the exact failure class that refactor exists
to eliminate, and this one survives the cutover untouched, since the
apply command clocks in through the same advised =org-clock-in=.


* MAYBE Give the review buffer a transient menu, once it has filters worth composing :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Apply
:ID:       118373e5-b89b-4001-ad52-1f68374745fb
:CREATED:  [2026-08-07 Fri 15:54]
:BLOCKER:  ids(720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1)
:END:

Captured 2026-08-07 from conversation, after pressing =h= in a live
=*org-review*= buffer and finding the help thin. Two cheap fixes landed
immediately — the mode docstring was rewritten as user-facing help
rather than maintainer notes, and =?= now calls
=which-key-show-major-mode= (with a =describe-mode= fallback, since
which-key is absent under =emacs --batch -Q=). This heading is the
*deferred* remainder.

*Availability, checked rather than assumed:* =transient= is loaded, and
=claude-code-ide= itself ships =claude-code-ide-transient.el= with 16
transient prefixes — so it is already the idiom in this exact
ecosystem, not a new dependency. =which-key= is loaded too. =hydra= and
=casual= are not installed.

*Why deferred rather than done:* transient earns its machinery when
there are *options and switches to compose* — that is what makes
magit's popups worth it. The review buffer currently has seven flat
commands and no state to toggle, so a transient menu today would be a
prettier =which-key= at roughly ten times the cost. =which-key= gets
most of the benefit for one line.

*What would change that*, and the real trigger to revisit: the filters
the buffer plausibly wants once it meets real volume — filter by
session, filter to only =(suggested)= items, mark-all-within-a-heading,
apply-with-confirmation. Those are switches, and transient handles
switches far better than a flat keymap does. The
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]] plan already
named magit-status's staging area as the model, so this is a genuine
direction — it should just follow the filters rather than lead them.
Blocked on =720b2dcf= being used enough to know which filters are
actually wanted; guessing at them now is how the machinery gets built
for a UI nobody needs.

*Promoted to =**= 2026-08-15, evaluated for cancellation, immediate
implementation, or promotion — and promotion is the honest answer.*

=720b2dcf= is =DONE=, so the =:BLOCKER:= is satisfied and this is no
longer *structurally* blocked. It stays =MAYBE= because its own trigger
has not fired: the condition written here is "filters worth composing",
and the review buffer still has flat commands with no state to toggle.
Nothing in real use has demanded a filter. The friction actually
encountered on 2026-08-15 was the assignment prompt
([[id:0d055205-a670-45c6-b4af-980961603a7c][0d055205]]) and candidate
ranking ([[id:49cbe319-f7db-413a-ade8-c4850c1fab8c][49cbe319]]) — neither
of which a transient menu would have helped with.

*Not cancelled,* because the direction is sound and cheap to revisit:
=transient= is already this ecosystem's idiom, =claude-code-ide= ships 16
prefixes of its own, and the trigger condition is written down precisely
enough that a future session can check it rather than re-derive it.

*Not built,* because this heading's own argument forbids it — "guessing
at them now is how the machinery gets built for a UI nobody needs." That
reasoning has not weakened; if anything the two defects found on 08-15
suggest the buffer's next improvements are correctness, not composition.

*Promoted rather than left in place* on the reasoning
[[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] has now applied
five times: this is a capability that epic *enables* rather than one it
*requires*, and an epic which keeps every downstream capability can never
close. With this and
[[id:0c8644ff-f7b1-4789-ac1e-babf2e9fbc8e][0c8644ff]] out, that epic's
children are 29 =DONE= and 2 =CANCELLED=, and it can close.


* MAYBE Let the review command ask Claude to explain the ambiguous gaps between guideposts :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Apply
:ID:       0c8644ff-f7b1-4789-ac1e-babf2e9fbc8e
:BLOCKER:  ids(5ff5a4b8-2fee-472a-b5e1-c9de8cf96704 406e9200-c3e7-49ac-a8d0-d6ff8e061ce4)
:CREATED:  [2026-08-07 Fri 08:05]
:END:
:PLAN:

Captured 2026-08-07 from conversation. The idea as posed: while
reviewing a heading in
[[id:720b2dcf-6af1-45f3-96a7-aa841e5651e1][720b2dcf]]'s command, Emacs
offers "Would you like some help with this one?" and, on approval, calls
back to Claude for a recommended aggregation of that heading's
guideposts.

*Transport, researched 2026-08-07 — the protocol answer is "yes, but not
here."* MCP's =sampling/createMessage= is exactly this primitive: a
*server* asks the *client* to run an LLM completion on its behalf, so no
server-side API key is needed, and the spec's own message-flow diagram
puts a human approval step on both the request and the response. It has
existed since protocol version =2024-11-05=, which is what
=claude-code-ide= declares. (Distinct from =elicitation/create=, added
in =2025-06-18=, which asks the *user* for structured form input rather
than the model.)

Three independent blockers, in increasing severity:

- =claude-code-ide.el= implements none of it — zero matches for
  =sampling=/=createMessage=/=elicit= across all ten of its =.el= files,
  and nothing upstream proposes it.
- More fundamentally, it has no *outgoing request* machinery at all. It
  can send fire-and-forget notifications
  (=claude-code-ide-mcp--send-notification=) but has no pending-request
  table and no id→callback correlation, so it cannot issue a JSON-RPC
  request and await a reply. (=--send-ping= is a websocket-level frame,
  not a JSON-RPC request — easy to mistake for evidence otherwise.)
  Adding sampling is therefore "build bidirectional request/response
  correlation," upstream, not "add a handler."
- Sampling only works if the *client* declares the =sampling=
  capability, which Claude Code may well not. Unverified.

*The architectural blocker that outranks all three:* sampling is
server→client over a *live session*. This refactor exists precisely so
the human can review whenever they choose — very often with no Claude
Code session connected at all. The practical transport, if this is ever
built, is shelling out to =claude -p= asynchronously: no protocol work,
no upstream change, works with nothing connected.

*Design objection, and a reframe that answers it.* Recommending an
aggregation is a more confident version of exactly what 720b2dcf
forbids. The danger is not that the model writes anything — it is
*anchoring*: a fluent, plausible-sounding decomposition is far harder to
reject than a transparently mechanical threshold, which degrades the
very safety property the review step exists to provide. Worse, on the
queue file alone the model would have *strictly less* information than
the threshold algorithm — the same timestamps and heading IDs, minus any
domain knowledge — so it cannot outperform the heuristic; it can only
add variance and unearned authority.

The version worth building instead: don't ask Claude to *recommend
intervals*, ask it to *explain the gaps*. Given the session transcript
as input, "what was happening between 10:02 and 10:35?" is a
retrieval-and-summarization task models are genuinely good at and no
heuristic can do at all — and it leaves the interval decision entirely
with the human. That reframing also changes the dependency: the
load-bearing question becomes whether the transcript is in scope as an
input, not whether Emacs can reach a model.

Sequence well after 720b2dcf ships and real queue data exists — there is
no way to judge whether the plain threshold is good enough until it has
been used in anger.

:END:
*That condition is now met, 2026-08-13, and the answer is no — but the remedy is smaller than this heading assumes.*

The threshold has been used in anger: 23 unattributed spans assigned by
hand in one sitting. The mechanical suggestion built for
[[id:3d0487f4-b8f8-451c-88bf-ab537663cbd0][3d0487f4]] (nearest preceding
=DOING=) managed 18 of 23 and was wrong in instructive ways — it latched
onto a =CANCELLED= heading for eight hours until taught to release, and
offered one heading for a 45-minute span that actually covered five.

*A third source of evidence, discovered by using it before proposing
it.* Asked "how should I identify what a span was circling?", the answer
that worked was neither the transcript nor a model: *git commits and
=:CREATED:= stamps falling inside the span's window.* Three shell
commands resolved three of four spans unambiguously — the 15:30--15:34
span brackets a heading created 15:33 and a commit at 15:34; the
12:36--12:59 span contains three commits and three heading creations.

*Why that beats both options above.* It needs no sampling, so every
blocker is moot. It needs no transcript, so the "is the transcript in
scope" question does not arise. And it answers the design objection
outright: commits and =:CREATED:= stamps are *mechanical evidence*, not
a fluent recommendation, so there is nothing to anchor on. The human
still decides; they simply stop running =git log= by hand. Filed as
[[id:5ff5a4b8-2fee-472a-b5e1-c9de8cf96704][5ff5a4b8]] — a *sibling*,
deliberately, since it shares the goal but none of the mechanism.

*What remains for this heading* is the residue mechanical evidence
cannot resolve: a span straddling two tasks, where commits exist on both
sides and only the narrative says where the boundary fell. The 54-minute
18:12--19:07 span was exactly that shape. So this stays =MAYBE=, its
scope narrowed from "explain the gaps" to "explain the *ambiguous*
gaps". The title was narrowed to match on 2026-08-13; until then the
narrowing lived only in this body, so =org_outline= and the agenda both
still advertised the original scope — which is how a heading gets picked
up later and built to the wrong size by someone reading the index rather
than the prose.

*Re-evaluated 2026-08-15* for cancellation, immediate implementation or
promotion. *Not cancelled:* the residue is real, identified concretely,
and nothing since has made it go away. *Not built:* judging its value
requires the mechanical version to have been used enough to show how
often the residue actually occurs. Six unassigned spans were sitting in
the review buffer that day, but they were unassigned because of
[[id:0d055205-a670-45c6-b4af-980961603a7c][0d055205]] — the assignment
prompt excluding headings created after the span — not because anything
was ambiguous. Evidence about a defect, not about this feature.

*The reframe, which matters more than the promotion.* This heading is
scoped to the *review command* — phase 1, queue → =:LOGBOOK:=. But the
question it answers, "what was happening between 10:02 and 10:35", is
the *attention pass* question — phase 2, =:LOGBOOK:= → attention
intervals, where the human reconstructs a day from evidence
([[id:4f8500e6-b468-4fd4-a0f6-ebf04c7a57ba][4f8500e6]]). That is where a
narrative explanation is wanted and where no heuristic can substitute.
The same conclusion was reached independently under
[[id:f90d745e-ccf9-49d6-a92e-df25c17b4dfb][f90d745e]] — *a recall cue,
not a metric* — which is exactly what this proposes. Re-point it at
phase 2 before building anything.

*One open question got a partial answer on 2026-08-15:* "is the
transcript in scope as an input?" It is readable at
=~/.claude/projects/<slug>/<session>.jsonl= and its structure is
countable — 276 =type:"user"= entries of which 25 were real prompts, in
one session. So the input exists and is parseable. Whether it *should*
be read remains a judgement, not a capability question.

The transport conclusion still stands and is worth not re-researching:
not MCP sampling — there is frequently no live session at review time —
but =claude -p= asynchronously.

*Three details checked 2026-09-01, none of which change the verdict.*

- *0d055205 is DONE,* so the caveat that discounted the six unassigned
  spans has been discharged: the review buffer's unassigned spans are
  now evidence about ambiguity rather than about that defect. The "not
  built" reasoning above asked for exactly this, so the count is worth
  looking at the next time the buffer is open.
- *The =:BLOCKER:= is half-satisfied.* 5ff5a4b8 is DONE; 406e9200 is
  =DOING= and still open, so the heading remains blocked.
- *The "promoted to =**=" note is moot.* Level-1 categories were
  retired, and this sits at level 1 again. The link to =9e627dc0= above
  was also re-pointed: that heading was divided on 2026-09-01 and its
  id travelled with the child, which is now a narrow DONE leaf rather
  than the conclusion being cited. The story is f90d745e.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/nested-gathering-wozniak.md][Plan]]

*Rewrite CLAUDE.md, =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= and the two
skills to describe the queue+review model rather than the live-driving
model they were written for.* Large enough that it is not a drive-by
edit tacked onto another task, and consumed in pieces: individual edits
land under it as they come up.

*The body was redistributed into children 2026-09-01,* at the user's
instruction, after growing to 369 lines of accumulated scope written
between 2026-08-10 and -08-17. It had become a backlog in prose — a
document nobody could act on, in a heading whose whole purpose is to
make documents actionable. Every scope item now has a child or a
recorded reason for having none. This body holds only what the group is
*for*.

*Twelve of the seventeen scope items were already done, and none of them
said so.* That is the finding worth keeping from the pass, and it is the
same one the [[id:b36e6369-a210-4540-adc7-0c0c188e9791][b36e6369]] slice is named for: the artifacts
had moved and the prose describing them had not. Verified against the
live files rather than recalled — culled as landed:

- *The four 2026-08-11 drifts,* all four fixed. The session hooks no
  longer claim to call =emacsclient=; power assertions are cross-linked
  to [[id:1a5a5254-f62d-46e7-9a6c-3d84f2f52873][1a5a5254]] as an
  attribution signal rather than dismissed as noise; a =DOING= heading
  with no running clock is documented as the expected steady state; and
  the =#+TODO:= template now matches TODO.org's line 1 byte for byte,
  with DONE.org carrying a =#+TODO:= line where it had none.
- *The =kill-emacs-hook= drift,* which went when the "Emacs integration"
  section was replaced by a pointer to the live Doom config — the fix
  being to stop duplicating it rather than to correct the copy.
- *The two hook directories,* now documented in the repository layout,
  including that =.claude/hooks/session-context.sh= sits apart "for no
  recorded reason" and that consolidation is open.
- *The =org-blocker-hook= sentence,* which now names both guards and
  what each blocks on, retiring the reading that "nothing enforces
  =:BLOCKER:= in this repo."
- *The =NEXT=-nomination rule,* shipped into CLAUDE.md by
  [[id:dd2399c0-52e2-463d-9423-e7d7a08f3b12][dd2399c0]] — and
  deliberately *not* into the org skill, which
  [[id:befaed0a-18ed-445e-b997-8b9c2965ad45][befaed0a]] settled the same
  day: path-scoped rules hold org-*file* conventions, CLAUDE.md holds
  ways of working.
- *The post-cutover vocabulary* — per-turn *guideposts* rather than
  per-turn *clocking*, and the point that churn relocates to the queue
  rather than disappearing. Landed with
  [[id:43d317e8-f2b6-4ce2-9634-64e9bda83efe][43d317e8]].

*One item was culled as a duplicate rather than as done:* "mechanically
check the checkable claims", scoped 2026-08-11, is
[[id:d2a0f54c-b59a-4855-ae3d-c93a6d3aa177][d2a0f54c]] — already =NEXT=,
already carrying the same list (every cited =:ID:=, defcustom and
function name resolves; every is-live claim has a greppable anchor), and
already naming =pre-commit= as its home. Its stated overlap with
[[id:d7849119-8224-4993-a8ab-83e1434df53c][d7849119]] stands there too.
Nothing is filed here for it; the work exists, one heading, in the
current slice.

*Six children were filed for what actually survived,* all of them
documentation gaps confirmed by grep on 2026-09-01 rather than assumed:
the file-header template's missing =#+STARTUP:=, the dormant
=:BLOCKER:= on a =MAYBE= heading, drawer-scoped timestamp reading, the
sibling-survey idiom, =load-file= not picking up a changed =defcustom=
default, and saying outright that CLAUDE.md is a starting point while
the artifact is the authority.

*The pattern under all of it, which the rewrite should state in its own
voice:* CLAUDE.md is loaded into every session by default while the
artifacts it describes are not, so a stale claim in it outranks the
truth until someone deliberately checks. That is filed as its own child
because it is the one item that does not get fixed by fixing anything
else.

*Not blocked.* All three =:BLOCKER:= ids — 7771fc63, e51d6ba1,
c084553c — reached =DONE= on 2026-08-14. This is unfinished because it
is a multi-part rewrite being consumed in pieces, not because anything
is waiting.

*Split out of
[[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] on 2026-08-14,* on
the reasoning that epic's body records twice: an epic that absorbs
everything downstream of the code it produced can never close. This was
its last non-=MAYBE= child, which is what
[[id:38b92521-bba4-4bb5-9cd9-465862471a72][38b92521]] had been waiting
on.


** DONE Correct CLAUDE.md's account of the queue architecture :code:

:PROPERTIES:

:CREATED:  [2026-08-14 Fri 19:59]
:CLOSED_SOURCE: git
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- [2026-08-14 Fri 20:10]--[2026-08-14 Fri 20:10] assigned at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-14 Fri 20:11]--[2026-08-14 Fri 20:15] =>  0:04
- State "DOING"      from "NEXT"       [2026-08-14 Fri 20:11] \\
  start CLAUDE.md correctness pass
- [2026-08-14 Fri 20:11]--[2026-08-14 Fri 20:11] assigned at review
- [2026-08-14 Fri 20:11]--[2026-08-14 Fri 20:15] correct CLAUDE.md architecture claims
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-14 Fri 20:15] \\
  all ten defects corrected and verified
- [2026-08-14 Fri 20:15]--[2026-08-14 Fri 20:15] assigned at review
:END:
:PLAN:

The factual pass. CLAUDE.md still files the whole queue design under a
heading reading "Direction (*not yet implemented*)" and still documents
the tools as acting on the buffer — =org_set_todo='s own docstring says
"Changes no TODO keyword", =org_clock_in='s says "Opens no clock". A
session reading CLAUDE.md today is told that calling =org_set_todo=
changes state. That is a false behavioural instruction, not a stale
detail, and it is why this child is =NEXT= rather than queued behind the
others.

Also in scope, each verified against source rather than inferred: three
registered tools missing from the table (=org_amend=, =org_outline=,
=org_review_apply=); =session-pause= described as "a thin alias for
=org_clock_out=" when it is =exec queue-append pause=; no mention
anywhere of =PermissionRequest=/=PermissionDenied=, =block_start=/
=block_end=, or the =.block-open= sentinel, all shipped in =c8c33a1=; a
paragraph at =CLAUDE.md= L518--524 left garbled by a botched edit; and a
repository layout omitting 7 of 10 =bin/= scripts and all 7 of
=bin/hooks/=.

*The =#+TODO:= line is the one with teeth.* CLAUDE.md's template omits
=REVIEW= — which =org_set_todo= accepts, and which appears nowhere in
CLAUDE.md at all — and omits every logging cookie. The real line is
=TODO(t!) NEXT(n!) DOING(d!) REVIEW(r!) WAIT(w@/!) MAYBE(m!)
| DONE(D!) CANCELLED(c@)=. The =@= means *prompt for a note*, so =WAIT=
and =CANCELLED= prompt while every other transition only timestamps: a
transition driven non-interactively through =emacsclient -e= hangs for
those two keywords and not the others. That is the exact hazard the queue
architecture exists to route around, and it is currently written down
nowhere. Record =REVIEW= as experimental
([[id:c954f650-b92f-4920-9967-ab6c44577b30][c954f650]]) rather than as
settled.

:END:
*Shipped 2026-08-14* (=871f415=), and the debrief is written 2026-08-24, backfilled during [[id:7fbab3b3-7843-4290-94a5-d421413f2be4][7fbab3b3]]'s sweep -- this heading closed before the convention requiring one existed.

All ten defects the body enumerates were corrected in CLAUDE.md in a single pass and verified against the live files rather than against recollection, which is what the state-change note "all ten defects corrected and verified" records.

*What the correction was actually about, since the title understates it:* CLAUDE.md described a queue architecture it did not have. The tool table said =org_set_todo= changed a keyword and =org_clock_in= opened a clock, when after the 2026-08-11 cutover all three queue an event and change nothing. A reader following the file would have expected a state change, seen none, and concluded the tool was broken -- which is why the corrected version now opens by saying so outright and tells the reader that a stale read is *expected, not a failure*.

*Written from evidence rather than memory,* and the distinction matters for a backfilled debrief: the commit, the two state-change notes and the body's own enumeration are the sources. Nothing here reconstructs what the pass "must have" done.

The org skill already documents TODO keywords, the tag table,
=#+ARCHIVE:=, and =:BLOCKER:=; org-dev already covers
=~/.config/doom/{config,init,packages}.el=. CLAUDE.md restates both, so
most of what looks like "chunking" here is deduplication with the skill
as the single source of truth.

Anything removed that the target skill does *not* already say gets added
there first — nothing is deleted without a home. Two things stay in
CLAUDE.md deliberately even though they look movable: the requirement
that the Emacs server be running, since every tool goes through
=emacsclient= and a session that learns this lazily has already failed;
and the note not to reintroduce =org-clock-persist-load=, which is a trap
with a real failure mode and belongs where it is read unconditionally.

Kept separate from its sibling because it changes *where* instructions
live rather than whether they are true, and it is the pass most likely to
be judged wrong later.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/nested-gathering-wozniak.md][Plan]] — the epic's
plan document, which carries this child's design as its second half rather
than a file of its own.

:END:
*Shipped 2026-08-14*, planned in Plan Mode and carried out the same evening -- =PLANNING= at 20:43, =DOING= at 21:22, =DONE= at 21:26. Debrief written 2026-08-24, backfilled during [[id:7fbab3b3-7843-4290-94a5-d421413f2be4][7fbab3b3]]'s sweep, since this heading closed before the convention requiring one existed.

The dedupe ran as the body describes: content CLAUDE.md was carrying that the org and org-dev skills already owned was removed from CLAUDE.md, and *both skills were corrected in the same pass* rather than merely being deferred to. That second half is what the state-change note records with "dedupe done, both skills corrected", and it is the part that makes the removal safe -- deleting from CLAUDE.md while trusting an uncorrected skill would have lost the content rather than moved it.

*The test that decided what stayed is worth restating, because it still governs:* a rule that must hold when no =.org= file is open cannot live in a path-scoped rule, since a rule that does not load is a rule that does not apply and the failure is silent. That is why the state transitions and the queue architecture stayed in CLAUDE.md while the file-header template and tag conventions left for =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md=.

*Written from evidence:* the commits, the three state-change notes, and the body's own scope. Nothing is reconstructed.

Blocked on both siblings deliberately: the decision takes the resulting
file size and shape as input, and the user's standing instruction is to
raise the question again *after* the content changes rather than predict
it now.

*The obvious mechanism does not work, which is why this is a decision and
not a task.* =@path= imports look like the answer and are not — per
Claude Code's memory docs, "splitting into =@path= imports helps
organization but doesn't reduce context, since imported files load at
launch." Imports organise; they do not defer. What actually loads lazily
is =.claude/rules/*.md= carrying =paths:= frontmatter, which loads only
when Claude reads a matching file, and skills, which load on demand.

If it goes ahead, org conventions under =paths: ["**/*.org"]= is the
natural first candidate. The caution is that =.claude/rules/= is unused
in this repo, and a rule that silently fails to load is indistinguishable
from one that loaded and was ignored — so adopting it needs a fresh
session to verify, not an assertion that it works.

The documented target is under 200 lines. CLAUDE.md is 640. This may
still close as won't-do.

*Decided yes, and shipped 2026-08-20* as =.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= carrying =paths: ["**/*.org"]=. It took the file header template, the keyword and tag policy, the top-level-headings-are-categories rule, and =:BLOCKER:= usage. CLAUDE.md keeps a pointer where the content used to be.

*The split test that decided what moved, and it is not size.* A rule that must hold when no =.org= file is open cannot live in a path-scoped rule, because a rule that does not load is a rule that does not apply and the failure is silent. So the state transition table, "create a heading for any newly described task", and the queue architecture all stayed in CLAUDE.md regardless of how much room moving them would have bought.

:END:
*Verified 2026-08-21 by the standard this heading set for itself* -- "adopting it needs a fresh session to verify, not an assertion that it works." A session with no prior knowledge of the file read =TODO.org= and the rule was injected unprompted at that moment, not at launch. That is the lazy-load behaviour the decision rested on, observed rather than asserted.

*Unblocks [[id:9ae4b17e-278c-482f-9114-df28b2254806][9ae4b17e]]*, which was waiting on exactly this confirmation: its body records that the path-scoped rule "turns out to be the *portability* mechanism, not merely a context-saving one", since a user-level =~/.claude/rules/org-conventions.md= costs nothing in repos with no org files.

a reader of the drawer actually needs: do not infer these numbers from a
drawer. They are why two CLOCK lines on one heading can describe
adjacent work and still be separate, and why a turn you remember taking
thirty seconds may appear nowhere at all.

*Verified:* =grep= for the three variable names in CLAUDE.md returns 3,
where it returned 0 on 2026-08-19 and still returned 0 this morning.
